- **A quit waits for every desktop store's writes, and both hosts' teardown waits for core's config writes** (`jsonConfigWritesSettled` and `allJsonConfigWritesSettled` in `src/core/config/json-config-write.ts`, `flushCoreWrites` in `src/core/chat-panel/index.ts`, `settlePending` in `src/shared/settle-pending.ts`, `flushStores` in `src/desktop/main/index.ts`, `deactivate` in `src/vscode/extension.ts`). Each store that writes through `writeJsonConfig` has a `flush` that settles once its files' write queue is empty, asking the queue again after each round so a write queued while it waits is waited for too, and never rejects: `panels.json`, `window-layout.json`, `projects.json`, `trusted-folders.json`, `usage-warnings.json`, `state/global.json` and `state/workspace.json`, `secrets.json`, and the settings store's user file, every file it wrote and every loaded project's project and local files. The quit awaits them all together after the window closed and core's dispose, which can still write them, with `flushCoreWrites`, within the 10 s bound. `flushCoreWrites` settles every `writeJsonConfig` write in the process, asking again after each round: `~/.damocles/mcp.json` and `.damocles/mcp.local.json`, settings-file editor saves, setting writes, and permission rule writes and renames. VS Code's `deactivate` awaits it after `ChatPanelProvider.dispose`, even when that dispose fails, for at most 5 s, the time VS Code gives `deactivate` before it ends the extension host. Hot-exit backups flush the same way earlier, when the editors are released.
- **Desktop secrets are encrypted with the OS credential store, or kept in memory and the user told** (`platform/secrets-store.ts`, `src/core/volatile-secrets-notice.ts`). `SecretsStore.isPersistent` is false when safeStorage cannot encrypt (no encryption, or Linux `basic_text`); `withVolatileSecretsNotice` then warns once per run, on the first secret stored, that it lasts only until restart, with an action that opens the README's "Desktop credential storage". An entry that fails to decrypt (a locked keyring, a denied keychain) keeps its ciphertext in the file across later writes and is dropped only when its key is stored again or deleted, because a keyring unavailable for one run must not cost the user every saved token.
- **The renderer is untrusted.** Every WebContents (the window, each chat and page view, the overlay view and the notification popup's view) runs `contextIsolation: true`, `nodeIntegration: false` and `sandbox: true`, with `app.enableSandbox()`; three bridges, one per kind of page: a chat or browser page reaches main only through the panel preload's four-method `damoclesBridge`, the window page only through `damoclesShell` and the overlay view and the notification popup only through `damoclesOverlay`; no page gets a second bridge. `app.on('web-contents-created')` hardens every WebContents: popups are always denied (http/https go to `ShellService.openExternal`, which accepts only http and https and opens the canonical href); `will-navigate` and `will-redirect` are cancelled for anything but the exact `app://` page main loaded; `will-attach-webview` is prevented. Permissions are denied by default, media and notifications included. The single exception is `clipboard-sanitized-write` (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`, for the copy buttons), granted only to a registered panel view's own webContents, main frame, exact `app://damocles` origin, through one predicate shared by the request and check handlers; `clipboard-read` stays denied, and device permissions are denied. No paste path reads the clipboard into a renderer without a user action main observed itself (`src/desktop/main/clipboard-gestures.ts`): a key or mouse press in `before-input-event` or `before-mouse-event`, an overlay pick, or an application-menu click or accelerator. Synthetic DOM events and Playwright's CDP keys never reach those hooks, so tests press keys with `webContents.sendInputEvent`. The window page can still run commands in a terminal it creates, so keeping script out of that page remains the real boundary. The `app://` handler serves only files that stay inside `dist/webview` or `dist/desktop-shell` after decoding, resolution and realpath (plus the single app icon file), and in-memory panel pages by exact panelId plus the shell, overlay and notifier pages at their exact paths; anything else is a 404, including the built `index.html` files, which carry no CSP (every page is generated in memory under one). A script response carries its own `default-src 'none'; script-src app://damocles`, because a worker does not inherit its page's CSP. Panel IPC uses fixed channel names registered on each view's own `webContents.ipc`, which only that view's messages reach, so the renderer never carries its panel id (a process argument would be wrong once Chromium reuses a renderer process across same-site views); each handler still accepts a message only from that view's webContents, main frame, on its own `app://` page URL, and JSON-normalizes and bounds the payload before core sees it. A refused `init` answers null and the preload asks again on its next call. `EditorService` never launches a file: an approval diff shows in the asking chat's read-only overlay and everything else in the editor pane, both built by main. The shell's file IO goes only by a main-issued id or by a project key plus a relative path, and core confines a mention again through `Platform.confinement` (`resolveMention` refuses a file outside the chat's folder and never realpaths a named file itself). `ShellService.openFolder` opens only a folder, on both hosts, and `openExternal` opens only http and https in the canonical form on both hosts (`src/core/web-url.ts`), so a model-named path or a third-party URL can never launch a local handler. The window page opens the application menu only after a primary click or an activation key (Enter as it goes down, Space as it comes up) that main observed in that page: a one-shot grant in `ShellHost` (`isPrimaryClick`, `isActivationKey` in `clipboard-gestures.ts`), cleared when the page loses focus. A page script's call alone is logged and opens nothing.
- **The login shell environment is merged once, before anything spawns or connects** (`src/desktop/main/shell-env.ts`). On macOS and Linux the app runs `$SHELL -l -i -c` once, in its own process group with a 10 s deadline that kills the group, and reads the environment its own binary prints (running as Node) between random markers, so profile noise on stdout cannot corrupt it. Stdout is decoded as UTF-8 across reads (a non-ASCII `HOME` or `PATH` split between two reads stays whole), its 4 MB cap counts bytes, and the environment is taken as soon as the closing marker arrives, so a profile's background job that keeps stdout open neither delays the launch nor is killed. The command holds only the binary path and the marker; no environment value is ever interpolated into it. A failure is logged and the launch environment kept. Windows GUI apps inherit the user's environment and skip the merge. Then `probeGit` checks `git` once: its absence is one localized warning, and `setCheckpointGitAvailability` turns checkpoints and rewind off with that reason instead of failing per operation. The extension never sets it, so its checkpoints probe `git --version` per session as before.
- **A quit closes the window before anything disposes, and resumes on a new task after its prevented `before-quit`** (`src/desktop/main/index.ts`, `shutdown.ts`, `e2e/desktop/quit.spec.ts`, `e2e/desktop/shutdown.spec.ts`). `before-quit` is prevented while the quit asks about running terminals and unsaved editors (`releasing`; a cancel leaves everything running). Every close and quit shares the question under way, and every quit the quit under way, which resolves true when the user cancelled it (`QuitLifecycle` in `shutdown.ts`, VS Code's `pendingQuitPromise`), so a second Alt+F4, tray or Dock Quit while it asks opens no second dialog. A question a close or quit asks first shows the window, restoring it when minimized, since a window hidden to the tray would hide it and while it tears down (`disposing`). Once nothing can cancel it, nothing opens a chat or a page (`PanelViews.create` refuses while the chats close with their states kept, as for a window close and a host reload), and the window closes through its own close path. The settings modal is dismissed with it and follows none of its chats (`OverlaySettings.close`, `OverlayHost.dismiss`). The placement is saved and `panels.json` and `window-layout.json` are sealed (`seal`; a window opened again unseals them), as VS Code saves its window state on `onBeforeShutdown` and a closing window does not rewrite it (`windowsStateHandler.ts`), so the next launch gets the selection, chats and layout from before the teardown. Every chat then closes with its state kept, and main waits for its `closed` event and for every shell and overlay request it was still answering (`ShellHost.settled`, `OverlayHost.settled`); only then do the core, the terminals and the file watchers dispose and the stores flush, as VS Code's `onWillShutdown` follows the last window's close (`lifecycleMainService.ts`). No page can call into a disposed service: a request either arrived before the window closed and is answered against the live core, or never arrives. Nothing reopens the window meanwhile (a second launch, the tray, the Dock), and the shell is sent no chats-changed while a closing window or core closes the chats. The wait for the window and its requests is bounded at 3 s inside the 10 s teardown, after which the core and the terminals dispose and the stores flush anyway; once the quit can no longer be cancelled, a question a closing page was asking answers Cancel (`createMessageAsker` `closing`) instead of asking again in the OS message box, so its request settles. The whole teardown is bounded at 10 s, and then `app.quit()` runs again. Electron's `Browser::Quit` stores the handler's prevented result only after the handler returns, and a quit started in native code (Cmd+Q, the Dock, logout, CDP `Browser.close`) runs the handler as the outermost JavaScript frame, so its microtasks run first. A dispose that settles in microtasks would then call `app.quit()` inside the first quit, which overwrites it, so the second `app.quit()` waits for `setImmediate`. The log records each step (`[shutdown] quitting`, `[window] closed`, `Damocles core disposed`, `[shutdown] disposed; quitting`, `[shutdown] will-quit`); `window-all-closed` during the quit's own close is not logged, and the log sink closes at `will-quit`. A host reload keeps the window open while core is replaced. A shell, overlay or menu request that needs core runs through `CoreHost.run`, which hands it the running core at once, or during a reload the core the reload starts, and the request passes that core to everything it calls; only callers that cannot run without a core use `requireCore`. The launch's restore passes its core to the page restore too, and a chat closed while it was being restored, by the quit among others, gets neither a session nor its pages (`restoreInCore`, `restorePages`). A reload disposes the old core only after the requests running on it settled, waiting at most `CLOSE_TIMEOUT_MS` (3 s) and logging each one still running then, as VS Code's lifecycle joins pending work before a shutdown; a quit during a reload waits for that bounded drain inside its own 10 s bound. The teardown's first step is `CoreHost.stop()`, which refuses a request still waiting on a reload, so the window's requests settle before it closes.
- **Main-process failures reach the log, never Electron's error dialog** (`src/desktop/main/process-errors.ts`, `index.ts`). An uncaught exception or unhandled rejection in main is logged, as VS Code's main process does. The stores are read after the log sink is installed and inside the startup error handling, so what they skip is logged and a store that cannot be read ends in the startup error dialog. The log sink reports its own write failures once on stderr (a failed rotation keeps appending, a failed append drops that batch). A chat or window page that crashes more than three times in a minute is left dead and the user is told: a toast with Reload Chat for a chat, the overlay's dialog with Reload Window for the window page, which draws the title bar and sidebar. A toast raised before the window exists, while it is closed or before the popup window's page has loaded waits in main, and its timeout starts once that page shows it. `app.setName` runs before any path is read: `damocles` when packaged, `damocles-dev` unpackaged, so a dev build shares userData, the safeStorage key and the single-instance lock neither with every unpackaged Electron app nor with an installed Damocles. For the same reason the unpackaged app's AppUserModelID is `io.github.aizenvoltprime.damocles.dev`. Electron creates a Start Menu shortcut for the AppUserModelID only when the app calls `new Notification` or `Notification.isSupported()`, and the app calls neither, so no shortcut carries the ID and Windows takes the name and icon of a taskbar pin, the jump list and Task Manager from the running binary. The unpackaged app therefore runs on a branded copy of the Electron runtime, never the downloaded one: `dev:desktop` and `test:desktop` run `scripts/brand-dev-electron.mjs`, which copies `node_modules/electron/dist` to `dist-damocles-dev` with Damocles' icon and the product name "Damocles Dev" in `electron.exe`, as electron-builder brands `Damocles.exe`, and redoes it only when the icon or the downloaded binary changed. The copy keeps the name `electron.exe`, because Electron reports `app.isPackaged` for any other executable name, and lives in its own folder, because the shell caches an icon by path. `scripts/launch-desktop.mjs` and `playwright.desktop.config.ts` select it with Electron's `ELECTRON_OVERRIDE_DIST_PATH` (`devElectronDist` in `scripts/dev-electron-binary.mjs`), never Playwright's `executablePath`, which drops the preload Playwright adds to the app it launches. A host reload (`core-host.ts`) joins a reload already running, starts only once the chats being reopened into the running core are back (`CoreHost.openTabs` is the only caller of `openInitialChats`: the launch, a reopened window and the reload itself), starts no core once the quit stopped the host, and still starts a new core when the old one fails to dispose.
- **Chats stay loaded by retention, and leave memory only by retention or Delete** (`chat-pool.ts`, `index.ts`, plan AD8). `chatsToUnload` is a pure function over the loaded chats: it keeps the selected chat, every active chat (running, waiting on the user, `background` true, holding browser pages or restoring them), every restoring chat and the 3 most recently viewed idle chats that hold a session file, and unloads the rest; a chat with no session file is never unloaded, except an empty new chat (no conversation and no file), which is dropped once the selection leaves it. A chat core is still setting up is neither idle nor empty. A chat is restoring while its saved state (`DesktopPanel.savedSessionId`, the session its `panels.json` entry names) names a session core has not bound, because core registers a restored chat and reports its activity before the webview's `ready` claims that session: one rule (`sessionIdOf` in `index.ts`: the bound session, else the saved one) makes it neither idle nor empty and lists, selects and persists it under that session id, never as a `new:` chat, until core binds it or the webview's state drops it (a refused claim starts a fresh conversation, and each change of the saved session re-runs retention and the list). Retention runs after every selection, activity event, settled turn and view change, and each unload waits in that chat's work queue (`ChatWorkQueue`), behind any selection, rename, tag or delete of it, and runs only if the pool still says so then. An unload is the existing close path (dispose, drain checkpoints, release the lease), which also deletes the chat's `panels.json` entry. `background: true` keeps a chat until a later activity event reports it false; main never times it out. Selecting an unloaded chat checks `leaseRefusalFor` first: another process's lease shows the refusal toast and the selection does not move. Otherwise main creates a view restored with `{ sessionId, workspaceFolderKey }` and the webview's `ready` resume path binds it, through `claimStoredSession` like every other bind. Quit, window close and a host reload close every loaded chat in core's order with the states retained, so the persisted selection and each chat's saved pages stay as they were; no chat or page opens meanwhile, and a window close or quit seals `panels.json`: a closed selected chat then selects nothing, and the next launch restores every saved chat, shows the saved selection (by panel, then session, then project) before any loads and loads it first. A mention or Add to Chat for a chat main is still restoring (`chatRestores`, recorded for every chat: those main restores, `openInitialChats`, New Chat and selecting an unloaded chat, and those core opens itself, Open Chat, a fork or a host prompt with no chat loaded (`openCoreChat`), which wait for `PanelManager.whenSetUp`) waits until core restored it and its page is ready, then delivers to it; when core restored it without registering it, a toast says the chat could not be loaded. A restored background chat lays out when first selected, because Chromium sends a view's size to its page only once the view has been visible (a never-shown view's page reports a 0×0 viewport whatever its bounds). Main remembers in memory the last chat viewed per project; selecting a project selects it while it exists, else a new chat in that project, so the title bar always names the selected project and chat. A chat's project is its folder's while the project list holds that folder, else the default target's, since core sets the chat up or moves it there (`projectOf`); the selected project follows the selected chat on every view change and folder registry change, so main never publishes a project its own list refuses.
- **One process-wide network dispatcher, installed before the first connection** (`src/desktop/main/network/`). `installCaCertificates` sets Node's default CA list to the bundled roots plus the OS store (`tls.getCACertificates('system')`, which returns the OS store on Electron 44) plus the `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` file, read after the login shell merge and deduplicated by fingerprint. In the packaged app Electron strips an inherited `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` (see "Desktop packaging and updates", fuses). `installProxyDispatcher` installs an undici dispatcher with `setGlobalDispatcher`, plus undici's `fetch`, as pi's CLI `configureHttpDispatcher` does, and with its settings (pi's `httpIdleTimeoutMs`, `allowH2: false`, CONNECT tunnels, the ignored `error` listener). pi's library entry never calls `configureHttpDispatcher` (only its CLI `main()`, `rpc-entry` and interactive mode do), and nothing in the desktop process may, or it would replace this one. When `HTTP(S)_PROXY` is set it governs every origin through undici's `EnvHttpProxyAgent` (with `NO_PROXY`), because Chromium ignores those variables on Windows and macOS. Otherwise each origin asks `session.resolveProxy`, which applies the OS settings, PAC and WPAD, and takes the first route undici supports (`PROXY`, `HTTPS`, `SOCKS5`, `DIRECT`); `SOCKS` (v4) and `QUIC` fall back with a logged reason. Answers are cached per origin for 60 s, dropped once expired at the next lookup, and cleared on `powerMonitor` `resume`, because Electron exposes no network-change event. Logs name a proxy by scheme, host and port only, never its credentials. `safe-fetch.ts` validates every hop before it calls `fetch`, so its SSRF guard applies unchanged through a proxy. NTLM and Kerberos proxy authentication are not supported.

- **The shell renderer is the window's own page and reaches main only through `window.damoclesShell`** (`src/desktop/main/shell.ts`, `src/desktop/preload/shell.ts`, `shell-channels.ts`). Main generates `app://damocles/shell/index.html` on every load with a fresh script nonce (CSP `default-src 'none'`, nonce `script-src`, no inline handlers) around the built `dist/desktop-shell` assets. It draws the title bar (logo, breadcrumb, sidebar and theme toggles, and on Windows and Linux the window controls, which send a `WindowControl` main validates against `WINDOW_CONTROLS`; macOS draws its traffic lights) and the sidebar's Projects and Chats. The shell preload exposes project, chat (`chats:list`, `search`, `select`, `new`, `rename`, `tag`, `delete`), window (app menu, theme, sidebar toggle and show), overlay request, chat slot and browser page bounds, sidebar layout, grid sizes and focused-part calls and has no access to the panel or overlay channels. Every `damocles:shell:*` handler accepts a call only from the window's own webContents, main frame, on the exact shell URL (`isPanelSender`, read at call time). Chat ids are issued by main (a session id, or `new:<panelId>` for a loaded chat with no file). Project keys, `new:` ids and the chat a select names are resolved against main's own lists; a stored chat id for rename, tag or delete is resolved by the catalog, which accepts only one safe path segment that `StorageManager.folderOf` finds among the open folders' session files; ids and searches are bounded to 200 characters, a name to 1..200 and a tag to 1..50 after trimming, an overlay request is rebuilt by `parseOverlayRequest`, the app menu anchor and the chat slot must be finite, non-negative and below 100000 CSS px, the sidebar layout (`sidebar-layout`) and the grid's sash sizes (`grid-sizes`) arrive as separate reports, each taken only whole and within its minimums and merged by main, and the focused part is only `sidebar`, `editor`, `terminal` or null. Every refused select, rename, tag or delete reaches the user as exactly one toast (core shows the lease refusal), so the shell shows nothing of its own; a rename, tag, delete or popup request that fails in main is reported the same way and resolves (`reportFailure`), a popup the overlay could not show as dismissed. A chat list that fails to load is the one failure the shell shows itself, with a retry, and it never leaves another project's chats on screen. A chat list or search names the `ShellState.revision` its project key came from, since main may change the project list while the request is on its way (`ShellHost.chatList`): a key that state did not name is refused, and a key it named that main's list no longer holds answers null once main has sent the state without it, which the shell waits for, listing the project that state selects; main never answers null for a project its own state still names.
- **The overlay view is the window's top-most view and reaches main only through `window.damoclesOverlay`** (`src/desktop/main/overlay.ts`, `src/desktop/preload/overlay.ts`, `overlay-channels.ts`, plan AD1). Menus, dialogs, the settings modal and the notification center render in one transparent `WebContentsView` per window at `app://damocles/overlay/index.html`, which main generates like the shell page (nonce `script-src`, `font-src app://damocles`, `img-src app://damocles data:`, `base-uri` and `form-action 'none'`, no workers) around `dist/desktop-shell/assets/overlay.js` and `overlay.css`; the shell build fails when any page's fixed-name asset is not emitted, because Rollup emits identical stylesheets once. It is not a registered panel view, so it holds no permission, and it loads without taking focus. Main owns its two bounds modes: `hidden`, and `full`, the whole content area at the shell's zoom while a popup is open, so the shell's CSS-pixel anchors land unchanged; toasts never render here (D52's popup window). It restacks last after every view is added or moved up (`PanelViews` calls `onRestack`), so nothing draws over it. A popup is a typed request main validates field by field and rebuilds (closed `OVERLAY_ICONS` allowlist, at most 100 items, bounded labels, ids, tags and dialog text, finite coordinates); main issues its id, focuses the overlay only while the window is focused (otherwise it flashes the taskbar button and focuses the overlay from the window's `focus` event, `focusOnActivation` in `index.ts`) and forwards it. That flash and pending focus end when the last popup closes (`popupsClosed`, called before focus returns, since focusing a view can activate the window) or the window does, so a popup answered before the window activates leaves neither behind. The overlay page acknowledges a request as its handler receives it, before rendering it, and must do so within 2 s or main hides the overlay, logs it and rejects every open request; an acknowledged popup never times out. The acknowledgement proves only that the page is loaded, mounted and answering: a first render can take seconds on a cold or starved renderer (a first launch of freshly built files, CPU contention), and a deadline on the render would hide a popup about to show, so the user's click would do nothing and the popup's own requests would find it closed. What waits on the popup being on screen (the bell marking its entries seen) runs once the page reports it shown, after Vue rendered it, with no deadline. Main counts this deadline, and the rasterize one, in 250 ms ticks of its own timer rather than wall-clock time: a block of main's event loop costs one tick, so an acknowledgement that arrived during the block still counts, where a single 2 s timer would fire first and drop the user's answer. One popup is open at a time: a second request dismisses the first, except that a `message` dialog (main's question, never the shell's) stacks above whatever is open and nothing but its own answer closes it. An answer counts only for an open request, and only of its kind (a menu answer names one of its enabled items, a tag is trimmed and 1..50 or null); a malformed answer dismisses the request, since the overlay closed its popup before answering. When the popup closes, focus returns to the webContents that asked, never to the overlay itself: a hidden view keeps keyboard focus, so whenever the overlay hides while it holds focus and no such page is left, main moves focus to the selected chat. An overlay popup answers main only after its exit: menus, confirmations and the tag picker leave through a `TransitionGroup` whose after-leave sends the answer (a leaving popup is inert and aria-hidden), and the settings modal's exit is a Web Animation whose `finished` sends `{ kind: 'settings', closed: true }`, at once under reduced motion, so main's switch to `hidden` never cuts an exit short. A crash hides the overlay and rejects every open request. Main follows the page's life from its own webContents events, never from its own loads alone: a committed main-frame navigation (`did-navigate`: a reload by any route, DevTools, Ctrl+R in a dev build or Chromium itself) is a new document holding none of the old page's popups, so it rejects every open request and drops pending rasterizations as a crash does, and main sends the page nothing until `did-finish-load`. A request made while a page is loading (the first load, a crash's reload, any other reload) is held and sent once it has loaded, one held popup at a time as one is open (a later request dismisses an earlier one; a `message` dialog stacks), and is rejected only when no page will load: a crash past the reload limit, a main-frame `did-fail-load` other than `ERR_ABORTED`, or the window closing. A request with no page loaded or loading is refused. The shell page and the popup window's page likewise take no push from main between a reload's commit and its load. Its `damocles:overlay:*` channels live on its own `webContents.ipc` and accept a call only from that webContents, main frame, on the exact overlay URL. Native `Menu.popup` stays only for the application menu, which the title bar logo opens where the menu bar is hidden. The first popup shown after each overlay page load is logged with its acknowledgement and render times.
- **The notification center holds the run's notifications, and main owns every toast's life** (`src/desktop/main/notification-center.ts`, `preload/notifications.ts`, plan AD10, D26). Entries are structured data (kind, chat, project, summary, outcome, timestamps); every renderer localizes them. Each new `pendingPrompts` id of a chat raises one entry, once per core panel and prompt id: a prompt a team agent raised is `team` whatever it asks, an `input` prompt is `question`; a prompt that resolves withdraws its popup and flash and keeps its entry, and closing its chat, or a core reload for every chat, does the same for all its prompts and forgets their ids (`panelClosed`). A settled turn raises `done` only when its chat is not selected or the window is unfocused, `error` for an error or a rate limit (which names the full window and its reset: the 24-hour time when it is today, else the weekday, or past a week the date, with the time; with no window, no time), `limit` for a budget stop, and a usage crossing raises `limit` under Usage (next bullet). Core's non-modal notices are entries and popups too and keep the `NotificationService` answer (an action label or undefined). Lives are the kind table's (12 s, `done` 7 s, `limit` 9 s; notices 8, 12 or 20 s, 120 s with actions); main's timer pauses while the popup page reports a toast held (pointer or focus), a card that collapses into the "N more" pill releases its hold, and a reloaded page that main replays the toasts to starts with none held. The 2px life bar is a transform keyframe paused the same way and hidden under reduced motion, where main still times the toast. Do not disturb (global `KeyValueState`) and Notify me off silence the seven kinds' popups, every popup's sound and the flash; entries still collect and count, and notices still pop up. Both take one path (`popupPolicyChanged`), so turning either on also drops the seven kinds' popups already showing and stops the flash. The bell's state rides on `ShellState.notifications`; the center is an overlay request with only an anchor, opening it marks every entry seen once the overlay reports it shown, its clear and Do not disturb channels act only while it is open, and a row's answer names an entry id main issued. An action selects the chat (loading it, a lease refusal shown as its toast), then posts `focusAttention`, `openTeamOverlay` or `runChatCommand` to that chat's own view, only once the view is ready, only while that chat is still the selected one (`runEntryAction`), and after the `panelFocused` its show posts, which focuses the composer. An entry turns read when its action runs, when its chat is selected while the window has focus, or when the window gains focus on it; one raised for the chat being looked at arrives read (D52).
- **A usage warning shows once per window reset, across launches** (`usage-warning-store.ts`, D54). `<userData>/usage-warnings.json` holds `{ version: 1, warnings: [{ provider, windowId, resetsAt, threshold }] }`, one record per provider window, with a `windowId` of at most 100 characters (a longer one is never recorded). A record matches a crossing whose reset time is within core's 60 s tolerance (`sameReset`), and a recorded 95 covers 80. The center records a warning as it raises it, silenced or not, since its entry collects. Expired records are dropped on read and on each write; a warning with no reset time is never stored, since nothing says when its record would expire and core re-arms it within the run. A corrupt file is logged, ignored and replaced on the next write.
- **Desktop popups are the app's own window, not OS notifications** (`notifier.ts`, `overlay/NotifierApp.vue`, `overlay/chime.ts`, D52). Windows' notification switch and Focus Assist therefore do not hold them back; there is no Action Center history, and a Wayland compositor may ignore the position. Every toast, the seven kinds and core's notices alike, shows only in one frameless, transparent, always-on-top window at the bottom-right of the primary display's work area, whether or not the main window is focused; the app draws no toast inside its window. It shows inactive and loads with `focusOnNavigation: false`, so a popup never takes focus (only F6 focuses it), is sized to the stack its page reports and hides when empty. Its window type (`POPUP_WINDOW_TYPES`) is `panel` on macOS, non-activating and floating over full-screen apps on every Space (`setVisibleOnAllWorkspaces` is not used, because it hides the Dock icon), `toolbar` on Windows, which keeps it out of Alt+Tab and Task View, and none on Linux, where Electron 44 aborts on X11 creating a window with a type. On Windows and macOS the shown window ignores the pointer with forwarding (`setIgnoreMouseEvents(true, { forward: true })`) except while the page reports the pointer over a toast or the pill (`damocles:overlay:toasts:pointer`), so a click beside the toasts reaches what is under the window; forwarding is off while the window is hidden. On Linux the window's shape is the toast and pill boxes the page reports in its toast area message (`setShape`), which clips their shadows. The page is the overlay bundle at `app://damocles/notifier/index.html` (`NOTIFIER_PAGE_PATH` in `preload/page-paths.ts`, which main's router and the bundle both read) with the overlay preload, rendering the `ToastStack` under the entries' ids and the lives main owns, and announcing each toast as its own node, removed with the toast, in one of two hidden live regions that stay rendered while empty: approval and question toasts and core errors assertively, every other one politely. Main answers only `getState` and the toast channels there, the pointer channel included, from that page's main frame. A page whose window quitting already closed still gets its `getState` answer, so main never throws while it loads, but its toast messages no longer count. A popup's action shows and focuses the window, then runs the entry's action. Focusing the main window never hides, moves or replaces a popup: it stays until its life ends, it is answered or dismissed, or its prompt resolves. Each popup that Do not disturb and Notify me let through plays a synthesized chime (rising for a prompt that waits, one note for a finished chat or info, falling for an error or limit; a burst plays its most urgent tone, by `CHIME_URGENCY` in `preload/notifications.ts`) while `damocles.desktop.notifications.sound` is on, and the window allows audio without a gesture. Main holds the most urgent tone for popups that arrive before the page loads and plays it once the page has loaded; it holds one only while the popup window exists, so popups that wait for the main window play no sound when they show. It opens only while the main window exists and closes with it, or when quitting closes it first, so it never keeps the app running; toasts raised meanwhile wait and show once the main window opens again.
- **The taskbar shows the bell's unseen count, and prompts flash it** (`taskbar-counter.ts`, `taskbar-badges.ts`, `notification-art.ts`, D52). Every count change applies once per frame: a drawn red badge (`9+` past nine) as the Windows overlay icon with a localized description that counts new notifications, since the count is the unseen count that opening the bell clears, and `app.setBadgeCount` on macOS and Linux (Electron derives `damocles.desktop` from the package name, the deb and rpm file's name), where a call that returns false is logged once. Electron makes one HICON from the image's 1x representation at whatever pixel size it has, and Windows draws an overlay icon at 16 px times the display scale, so the badge is drawn once at 16 px times the primary display's scale factor and held as that single representation. `TaskbarCounter.reapply` sets the count again for a new window, a change of that scale (drawn at the new scale), a UI language change (the description is localized) and an overlay page load or the page's first answer after a missed deadline since it loaded (a dot drawn while the page could not draw is replaced). Approval, plan, question and team entries flash the taskbar button while the window is unfocused, until it gains focus or every prompt that flashed resolves; a focus stops the flash only when something flashes. `index.ts` owns the flash for two reasons, these entries and an overlay popup waiting for the window to activate, and stops it once neither is left. The badge is rasterized by the overlay page, which has the fonts and a canvas: main builds the art from its own data (SVG and text runs, the colour checked as `#rrggbb`), issues the request id, waits 2 s (counted in ticks, as for an acknowledgement), and accepts only one complete PNG of exactly the asked size within 64 KiB (signature, IHDR first, every CRC, IEND last) before decoding it; anything else shows a plain dot drawn in main.
- **Every question the desktop asks renders as the overlay's dialog; the OS message box is only the fallback** (`src/desktop/main/message-dialog.ts`, `overlay/components/OverlayDialog.vue`, D41). Modal `NotificationService` calls, the trust question and the window crash prompt go through one `AskMessage`: a `message` request main builds and bounds (`parseMessageRequest`), shown by the same `alertdialog` the shell's confirmations use, on the overlay stack's modal layer so it paints and takes Escape above the settings modal. Focus starts on the first action, except a danger dialog and the trust question, which focus Cancel (Don't Trust); Enter activates the focused button, Escape and the scrim cancel, the exit plays before the answer, and focus returns to the control that asked once it is enabled again; when focus was inside a listbox, menu or dismissable layer, that is the control that opened it (its `aria-controls` owner), never the owner of an ancestor some other control merely controls. Main accepts the answer only from the overlay page for the id it issued, so no chat view can answer a trust question. `dialog.showMessageBox` asks only when the overlay has no page loaded or loading, crashes or reloads while the question is open, or misses the 2 s acknowledgement, or the question exceeds the overlay's bounds, and the fallback is logged. File and folder pickers stay native.
- **The desktop settings modal is the overlay attached to the selected chat** (`src/desktop/main/overlay-settings.ts`, plan AD2). The title-bar gear (`damocles:shell:window:open-settings`), Ctrl+, / Cmd+, (`MENU_IDS.openSettings`, File on Windows and Linux, the app menu on macOS), every in-chat settings link (`openAppSettings`) and core's `EditorService.openHostSettings` (which calls `WindowService.openAppSettings` with its caller's section, so the browser DevTools notice opens Tools & integrations) open one `{ kind: 'settings', section?, account?, generation }` request (`account` opens that Accounts row expanded; a chat that needs OpenAI sign-in asks for `openai`); a second open while it shows sends its section and account to the modal (`damocles:overlay:settings:target`), which goes there, and Escape belongs to the modal. Main attaches it to the selected chat's core panel with `PanelManager.attachView`, once core has set that chat up (`restoredCorePanel`, so a launch or host reload restoring it never makes the modal open a chat), opening an empty chat on the default project first only when no chat is selected (`promptTarget`), and re-attaches on every selection change except during a window close or quit, which dismisses it first (`OverlaySettings.close`), pushing `damocles:overlay:settings:attached { generation }` before the attach: the renderer resets its stores when the modal opens and on that push, so no other chat's or earlier session's values show or get written, and the attach itself posts the host prompts it moves into the view, which the view accepts from inside `attachView`. The modal sends `requestSettingsState` once when it opens and once per attachment, on both hosts, and no other request for state: core answers with everything its sections render (the chat's settings and models, every account's status, Explore, MCP, tools, image generation, the voice settings and the size of the voice files, and on a host that keeps settings files which of them apply), posting `hostCapabilities` first, and later changes reach it as pushes; its sections request nothing when they mount, so neither a search keystroke nor a section switch asks again. Core copies each message of a `SETTINGS_VIEW_MESSAGES` type the chat receives to the view (`damocles:overlay:settings:message`); replies to the view's own requests go to the chat too, so a This chat change updates the chat's composer. The view sends `damocles:overlay:settings:send { generation, message }`; main drops it unless the generation is the current attachment's (so a write meant for one chat never lands on another after the selection moved), it is JSON within 64 KiB, and its type is in `SETTINGS_VIEW_REQUESTS`, then `dispatchFromView` checks the allowlist again and runs the router with the chat's context. The allowlist holds settings requests only: no `ready` (the view bootstraps with `requestSettingsState`), no permission, plan or PiSession dialog answers, no trust request, no opener that takes a path (`voiceOpenModelsFolder` reveals only the voice models folder), no settings file load or save (Edit settings.json sends `openSettingsFileInChat`, which opens the scope's settings tab in the editor pane). Only message types are logged. `prefs:get` returns the `damocles.desktop.*` values and the language this run launched with (the Language row offers Restart now only for another one), and while the modal shows main pushes them again on each change of those keys (`damocles:overlay:prefs:changed`); the first read never overwrites a value the modal set after asking, and a push never one whose write main has not answered; `prefs:set`, `app:relaunch` and `layout:reset` are refused unless the modal is open, `prefs:set` takes only a key declared in `desktop-configuration.ts` (`Object.hasOwn`) with a value of its type and enum (`isDesktopSettingValue`) and writes user scope, and the other two take no arguments. The overlay answers only after its exit animation; main then detaches the view and returns focus.
- **The desktop display language is `damocles.desktop.language` and applies after a restart** (D23; `readLanguageSetting` and `launchLanguage` in `platform/localization-service.ts`). `start()` reads it from the user settings file before the app is ready, since Chromium fixes its UI locale at launch, and appends `--lang` only for `en` or `el`; `parseDesktopLanguage` (`desktop-configuration.ts`) is the one validator, and a missing, unreadable or broken file or any other value reads as `system`. The bundles then use the setting, or with `system` the language the chat webview stored, else the OS locale; a setting of `en` or `el` clears that stored choice at launch so the webview follows it. Restart now runs the normal quit and relaunches only when nothing cancelled it (`QuitLifecycle.relaunch`, which registers `app.relaunch()` on `quit` as VS Code's `relaunch` does), so layout, chats and editors come back through restore. `damocles.desktop.restoreLayout` off starts the next launch with the default `window-layout.json` and a new chat on the default project, dropping the saved chats; Restore default layout resets the file and applies it live. `damocles.desktop.notifications.enabled` off silences the seven notification kinds' popups as Do not disturb does, and `damocles.desktop.notifications.sound` off mutes the popups' chime; see the notification-center bullet.
- **Browser pages are editor tabs of their chat** (`src/desktop/main/browser-tabs.ts`, `views.ts`). There are no top-level browser pages: `views.create` refuses a page whose `PanelOptions.owner` is not a loaded chat. Only the selected chat's pages show as tabs; selecting another chat swaps them, and selecting the first again returns its tabs and its active page. A page view is laid over the page area the shell reports (`damocles:shell:browser-bounds`) only while its tab is the active editor tab, and is hidden otherwise. A page is a panel view with the same panel preload and `damoclesBridge`, its own channel pair with the same sender check, the same navigation, popup and permission hardening, and the core-assembled screencast HTML with `localResourceRoots: []` and its unchanged CSP. Host messages go out through `webContents.send` (structured clone, so screencast frame buffers arrive intact); webview messages are JSON-normalized and bounded; the page renderer's `setState` persists nothing. The page script picks `acquireVsCodeApi` or `window.damoclesBridge` at load (`browser-webview-script.ts`). The tab's navigation bar never reaches core directly: main turns each action into the webview message the page's own toolbar would post (`navigate`, `goBack`, `goForward`, `reload`, `pickElement`, `openDevTools`), so core's handlers and `isNavigableUrl` stay the authority. The address bar's text reaches core as typed from both hosts: desktop main only checks it (`isTypedAddress`), and the VS Code page toolbar sends it unchanged. Core reads it with `typedAddress` (`src/shared/typed-address.ts`), which refuses text longer than `MAX_BROWSER_URL_LENGTH` and keeps a typed scheme in lower case, and ignores any `upgraded` flag a message carries. Text counts as having a scheme only when the scheme is one Chrome's omnibox knows or is followed by `//`, so `user:pass@example.com` is a host with credentials, and `about:blank` matches in any case. Without a scheme it follows Chrome's typed navigation (`ShouldUpgradeToHttps`): an IP address, a single-label host, a name under a top-level name no public registry assigns (`UNREGISTERED_TLDS`) or an address with a port other than 80 opens over http, and anything else opens over https marked `upgraded`. For such an address core (`src/core/browser/https-fallback.ts`) loads the http address when the https load fails with a connection (refused, reset, closed, aborted, failed), TLS, certificate or empty-response error, and logs the fallback. A DNS failure, an offline network, a cancelled load and a timed-out load keep the error page: since Chrome 150 a typed address never falls back after a timeout (crbug.com/515265983), and there is no fallback timer. `isNavigableUrl` stays the final check in main and in core. Both hosts' address bars mark an http page not served from this computer Not secure (`isNotSecurePage`, same module). Open in system browser passes only the URL main observed, http and https only, to `ShellService.openExternal`; when that is refused or fails, main raises one toast, and the shell catches every rejected browser channel.
- **Main applies the bounds of the shell's slots** (`views.ts`, `browser-tabs.ts`, `window-layout-store.ts`). The shell lays out the title bar, sidebar and the grid's slots and reports the chat slot's rectangle (`contentBounds`) and, while a browser tab is the active editor tab, its page area (`browser-bounds`); main converts each to DIPs, clamps it to the window, and places the selected chat's view and the active page on them. A pane that moves to another slot glides with a script animation, which fires no `animationend`, so the shell measures again once every glide has finished (`LAYOUT_SETTLED_EVENT` in `content-bounds.ts`), or right after the move with reduced motion. The window controls are HTML (`WindowControls.vue`), so Windows 11 shows no Snap Layouts flyout on the maximize button; Win+Z still opens it. Views stack chat < active page < overlay, restacked on every selection and tab change, since `addChildView` puts a newly created view on top. The window is frameless (`titleBarStyle: 'hidden'`; macOS `trafficLightPosition`). Windows and Linux have no `titleBarOverlay`: native controls paint above every view, so the overlay's scrim and modals could not cover them; the shell's `WindowControls.vue` draws them instead from `ShellState.windowState`, which main republishes on maximize, unmaximize and full-screen changes, and hides them in full screen, its menu bar is hidden on Windows and Linux with every accelerator still working, and its minimum size is 900x600. `<userData>/window-layout.json` (`writeJsonConfig`) holds the window's normal bounds, maximized and full-screen state and the sidebar layout; every field is validated on read and what is dropped is logged, a saved position on no display is clamped onto the nearest work area, and main owns `sidebarVisible` (Toggle Sidebar; Reveal in Files and the Search view's commands show it through a show-only path, `damocles:shell:window:show-sidebar` and `ShellHost.revealInFiles`, so a reveal never hides it) and the grid's slots, visibility and maximized pane. The shell owns the sizes while it runs: the sidebar reports only its width, section sizes and Search states (`sidebar-layout`; a project's state keeps the Replace input's text only while its Replace row shows, as VS Code saves `query.replaceText`, within `MAX_REPLACEMENT_LENGTH`), the grid only its sash sizes (`grid-sizes`), main stores each without republishing, and the shell takes main's sizes back only when `ShellState.layoutRevision` changes (Restore default layout), so a state push carrying older sizes never undoes a resize. A quit waits for its pending writes (see "A quit waits for every desktop store's writes"). A page tab's page sits in a rounded card inset from the pane; the shell reports the card's inner corner radius with the page bounds, and main rounds the page view to it (`View.setBorderRadius`, one radius for all four corners), so no square corner of the view covers a rounded corner in any layout.
- **Keyboard focus moves only for the user, except that a launch focuses its chat's composer once; no page outlives its chat** (`views.ts`, `browser-tabs.ts`, `index.ts`). Every chat and page view loads with `focusOnNavigation: false`, and create, reveal, restore, navigation and selection never focus a page view; only a user's action on a tab focuses it. A launch, a reopened window and a host reload show their chat with `focus: 'whenReady'`: `PanelViews` focuses that view once, on its `dom-ready` while the window is focused and not minimized, else when the window activates (Electron focuses the window's own page before emitting `focus`), and the webview's mount has already focused the composer. The user's first key or button press in any of the app's pages (`before-input-event`, `before-mouse-event`, observed in `index.ts`), another selection, a focus main gives the chat, or an overlay popup open at that moment drops it; a focus change never does, because the window's activation focuses its own page. A focus main gives a chat whose page has not committed waits for `did-navigate`, since the renderer drops earlier focus, and yields to focus reaching any other view first. Electron may focus the window's own page as the window activates, and AppKit does as it restores a minimized window, so main returns keyboard focus to the chat, page or overlay view that held it when the window was left (`activation-focus.ts`); a focus of the window's own page counts only while the window is active, a key or button press while it is inactive decides instead, and `focusOnActivation` wins. The window activates once per activation, at the first `focus` it reports itself focused (`isFocused()`) and not minimized at, and not again until it blurs or is minimized (`onWindowActivated` in `activation-focus.ts`), and deactivates when it blurs or is minimized (`onWindowDeactivated`); only then do main's activation focus, the launch focus, the flash stop and the viewed chat's read marking run. A minimized window that another process restores with `ShowWindow(SW_RESTORE)` and `SetForegroundWindow` emits `focus` on Windows before `restore` while `isFocused()` is still false, and again once Chromium has activated it and focused a view itself, which can be the window's own page, so a focus main moved at the first would be lost. X11 sends `focus` and then `blur` to a window it is iconifying, and `webContents.focus()` activates, and so restores, its window on Linux; a focus gained while minimized activates the window once it is restored, unless a blur comes first. AppKit resigns a miniaturizing window's key status before `minimize` but reports its `blur` up to ~100 ms later, and a restore started in between makes the window key, focusing its own page, before that `blur` arrives; so minimizing deactivates the window too. A chat that unloads or is deleted closes every page it owns, and core closes the chat's pages as well, including a failed subagent's kept tabs, and refuses any that register later; a chat holding pages stays loaded. Application-menu accelerators are the only shortcut mechanism, so New Chat (`CmdOrCtrl+N`), Toggle Sidebar (`CmdOrCtrl+B`), Next and Previous Chat and F6 fire whichever view has focus. Close Editor (`CmdOrCtrl+W`) closes the active editor tab; its key runs it only while the editor (page views included) holds focus, and otherwise reaches the chat. F12 opens DevTools only while a page tab is the active editor tab. F6 and Shift+F6 cycle focus through the parts sidebar (while shown), editor (while it has tabs), chat, terminal (while its pane shows a terminal) and the desktop popups' window (while a popup shows), because Tab cannot leave a WebContents; the title bar is not a stop. The popup stop is a user action, so it focuses the popup window and its page, which focuses the newest toast's action (else its first control) with its focus ring showing; Tab stays inside the stack, and Escape, or the last toast going while it holds focus, brings the main window forward and gives focus back to the part F6 came from (the selected chat when it came from none). Popups focused by a click, or left for another window, leave focus where the OS puts it. The shell reports when its sidebar or editor holds focus and main reads the chat and page focus itself. A menu item is enabled by its command's precondition alone (`Command.enabled`, VS Code's `precondition`), which never reads keyboard focus, so opening the application menu from the title bar greys nothing out. Whether an accelerator runs its command is `keyFires` (`commands.ts`, as VS Code's `menubar.ts` runs an accelerator as a keybinding with its `when` clause): the precondition, `Command.keyWhen` and, while an xterm's input has focus, the skip list. Electron calls a menu accelerator only for a key the focused page left unhandled, and on macOS as a key equivalent too, so a key that does not run its command has already reached the page or the pty. An item with `registerAccelerator: false` only shows a key another owner handles (the chat items' Ctrl+Tab routing, the xterm's Ctrl+Up and Ctrl+Down) and never runs from it, because macOS registers its key equivalent anyway. A menu click acts on the active editor or terminal wherever focus is; F4, Shift+F4 and Toggle Query Details act on the active Search Editor from the menu. New Browser Page and every browser affordance do nothing while `damocles.browser.enabled` is off.
- **`panels.json` v2 holds the loaded chats and the selection** (`panel-state-store.ts`). `{ version: 2, selected: { projectKey, sessionId?, panelId? }, chats: [{ panelId, state, pages, activePage }] }`: each loaded chat's webview state and its browser pages (addresses in tab order and the active index). Fields an older file holds for the retired side pane are ignored. A v1 file migrates on read: tabs become chats, and the selected tab becomes the selected chat with the project and session its state names; the next write is v2. Every field is read with own-property checks and validated, and what is dropped (a malformed panel id, state, address or selection, a non-chat tab) is logged. Unloading a chat deletes its entry and keeps the selected project and session. A restart reopens every saved page as a tab of its chat through `BrowserService.restoreChatPages`, which honours `damocles.browser.enabled` and refuses a non-navigable address per page. A quit waits for its pending writes (see "A quit waits for every desktop store's writes").
- **Desktop settings come from three files, and a folder's project and local files apply only while that folder is trusted** (`src/desktop/main/platform/settings-store.ts`). `damocles.*` keys sit flat beside `permissions` in user `~/.damocles/settings.json` (always), project `<folder>/.damocles/settings.json` and local `<folder>/.damocles/settings.local.json`. Precedence is local over project over user over the `package.json` default, and `get` merges a plain-object value key by key, recursively, across those layers exactly as VS Code does (`mergeSettingValues` in `src/core/config/settings-file.ts`; the fake platform and the vscode mock merge the same way), while `inspect` reports each layer as written. `SettingsStore.get`, `inspect`, `update` and `scopeFile` take an optional `SettingsFolder` (`path`, and `personalPath` for a worktree chat, D34). A read made for a chat passes that chat's folder (D38), as VS Code's `getConfiguration(section, folderUri)` does: `settingsFolderOf(target)` in `folder-registry.ts`, which is undefined on the home target, so a chat with no project reads user settings and defaults only and its Workspace writes go to the user file, never to `~/.damocles/settings.local.json`. The folder always comes from core's `ctx.folder` or `instance.folder`, never from a message field. The keys read with a folder are `CHAT_SETTING_KEYS` (`src/core/config/chat-settings.ts`: budget, task budget, auto-compact, the thinking and effort defaults, the permission-mode and YOLO defaults, the team role models and efforts, subagent concurrency and the two notice switches); each declares `"scope": "resource"` in `package.json`, and `chat-settings.test.ts` fails when core passes a folder for any other key. Every other read is window-level and passes no folder, which reads the registry's default project (`defaultProjectPath`, the rule `WorkspaceFolderRegistry.defaultTarget()` applies), the selected project on desktop. For a folder, the project layer is `<path>/.damocles/settings.json` and the local layer `<personalPath>/.damocles/settings.local.json`, and both apply only while `personalPath` is trusted (trust matches the exact folder, so a trusted parent trusts no child), checked when the layers are read and again on every read; a trust grant re-reads them. Each folder's layers are cached and watched while the default project or a loaded chat uses it (main reports the loaded chats' folders through `ChatFolders`), and a folder no chat uses drops its cache and watchers. `inspect` reports only the layers that apply: an untrusted default project, no open project, or a user-only key leaves `projectValue` and `localValue` absent, because `updateConfigAtEffectiveScope` picks its write target from `inspect` and would otherwise route a settings toggle into a refused or untrusted file (YOLO and the permission mode would become unchangeable). That is the `SettingInspection` contract on both hosts: a store reports `localValue` only where `update(key, value, 'local')` can write it, so a toggle of a key the local file supplies writes the local file however many projects are open. VS Code reports and writes a `WorkspaceFolder` value only for a resource, so its store passes the folder as the configuration resource (`Uri.file(folder.path)`), which also gives multi-root workspaces their folder values; with no folder `localValue` is absent there. `update` to project or local rejects for a user-only key, an untrusted folder (the default project when none is given) and no open project. Writes from the settings UI land per section (D37), decided in core per setter, never by the renderer: This chat sets the panel's own state; Workspace (`setBudgetLimit`, `setTaskBudget`, `setAutoCompact`) writes `local` for the chat's folder (desktop `.damocles/settings.local.json`, VS Code the folder's settings), or user on the home target, and core refuses it with a localized reason while that folder is untrusted (on both hosts, as the MCP exposure scopes do); `settingsUpdate` carries `workspaceWritable`, so the modal shows those rows disabled with a notice, offering the chat's `setProjectTrusted` where the modal sits in the chat page and naming the Projects list's trust action in the desktop overlay, which may not send it; every other setter writes user. `updateConfigAtEffectiveScope` (`settings-manager/utils.ts`) takes that home scope and the chat's folder for a per-chat key, and writes to the highest file that already holds the value when it ranks above home, so a change always takes effect; `damocles.desktop.*` keys are written only through the overlay's `prefs:set`, at user scope. After each write core posts `settingWriteResult` (`message-router/setting-write.ts`) under the key of the row that asked, with the scope and file the value the setter wrote now comes from (each setter returns the key it wrote and its home scope, so a setter that writes a key other than its row's reports where that key landed, and a write that leaves its key at the default reports that home scope and no file), or the error, an invalid value included, which the settings rows show as Saved to or an inline failure. Every write goes through `writeJsonConfig`, project and local ones with `confineTo` their `.damocles`. A file that stops parsing (a trailing comma, a `//` comment) keeps serving the layer it last parsed, and the user is told once, naming the file, until it parses again; an existing file read as empty while it held settings is taken as an editor mid-write and read again after 300 ms before it counts as empty; an unreadable path (EACCES, a directory) keeps the last layer too. Without that, one typo read as `{}` flipped every setting in the file to its default and back, `permissionMode` and tool availability included. The user file and every open project's two files are watched; an external edit of any loaded folder's files, a trust grant, a default-project change and a project list change fire `onDidChange` for each key whose layers changed. `search.useIgnoreFiles`, `useGlobalIgnoreFiles` and `useParentIgnoreFiles` default to VS Code's defaults (true), which a user or project value overrides as in VS Code. A `damocles.*` key in a `.damocles` file affects desktop only; the extension keeps VS Code's settings. The settings modal shows the file behind each value a project or local file supplies (`settingSources` on `settingsUpdate`, each entry the file from `SettingsStore.scopeFile` and the value it holds): a row's source badge names it, and the Workspace section lists every such key that no row of a shown section writes, with its value, its file and an Open file action that goes the way of the footer's Edit settings.json (`openSettingsFileInChat`); for a per-chat key the chat's folder's file, for every other key the default project's, because that is what applies. Desktop-only keys (`damocles.desktop.*`; now `theme`, `reduceMotion`, `language`, `restoreLayout`, `notifications.enabled` and `notifications.sound`) are declared in `src/desktop/main/desktop-configuration.ts`, an erasable table with no imports so that `scripts/generate-settings-schema.mjs` can import it. The store merges them into its contributed set (`withDesktopConfiguration` in `settings-store.ts`) with their defaults, an `application` key as user-only. They never enter `package.json`, so VS Code's Settings UI never lists them; the settings JSON editor's schema lists them in the user variant and flags the user-only ones in the project variant.
- **Project and local files never supply a user-only key** (`src/core/config/contributed-configuration.ts`). The set is derived from `package.json` at load, never hardcoded: `capabilities.untrustedWorkspaces.restrictedConfigurations` plus every key whose `scope` is `machine` or `application` (VS Code likewise never reads those from workspace settings; `machine-overridable` keys may come from a project). On desktop the `application` keys of `desktop-configuration.ts` join it, `damocles.desktop.terminal.profiles` among them. An entry that is such a key, a prefix of one or under one (`"damocles.compass": { "enabled": true }`) is dropped and logged, trusted or not. Known residual, as in VS Code: `damocles.browser.devToolsPort` is neither restricted nor machine scoped, so a trusted project may turn on the unauthenticated DevTools port.
- **Host dialogs render in a chat's webview** (`src/core/chat-panel/webview-prompts.ts`). Electron has no native text or list prompt, so desktop `DialogService.inputBox` and `quickPick` post `extensionUiRequest` (`password`, `items`) to the last active chat, or open a chat in the selected project and wait for its `ready` when none is loaded (the overlay renders menus and confirmations the shell asks for, not host prompts). A chat still being opened or restored counts as loaded: `PanelManager` registers a panel only once its session exists, well after its webview shows its input box, so `promptTarget` waits for the panels in `opening` before it opens one, which also makes prompts asked at once share one new chat. Request ids carry the `host-prompt:` prefix, distinct from PiSession's `<sessionId>:ui:<n>`, and `extensionUiResponse` is offered to `WebviewPrompts` first. An answer counts only from the panel the prompt was posted to, and a quick pick answer only when it names an offered item. An abort posts `extensionUiCancel` and resolves undefined; the panel closing resolves undefined; a webview restart re-posts the panel's pending prompts on `ready`. While a settings view is attached to the panel (`PanelManager.attachView`, the desktop overlay modal), its host prompts render in that view instead: each pending prompt records where it shows, and every attachment change (`onDidChangeAttachment`, `WebviewPrompts.resurface`) cancels it on the old surface and posts it on the new one, back to the chat on detach. The view's equivalent of the chat's `ready` re-post is `requestSettingsState`, which re-posts only the prompts shown in that view. An answer counts only from the panel the prompt was asked in and only from the surface showing it now, and one that arrives through the view (`ctx.view`) is offered to `WebviewPrompts` only, never to a PiSession dialog. PiSession's own dialogs are never redirected and never copied to a view, which is why `extensionUiRequest` and `extensionUiCancel` are `SETTINGS_VIEW_PROMPTS`, outside `SETTINGS_VIEW_MESSAGES`. Answers are never logged: they carry API keys and pasted OAuth redirect URLs, and `panel-attach.test.ts` enters a sentinel key through a view and checks the log. The VS Code host keeps `showInputBox` and `showQuickPick`.
- **Desktop opens files and diffs in the editor pane, and only approval diffs in the chat (D15)** (`src/desktop/main/platform/editor-service.ts`). Core threads the originating panel into every `EditorService` call (`OpenFileOptions.panelId`, `DiffRequest.panelId`), and the VS Code host ignores it. A `showDiff` with `approvalId` (the `toolUseId` of the pending `requestPermission`) posts `editorShowDiff` to the asking chat without revealing or focusing it (`ChatTabMessenger.deliver`; its notification entry is the way there), where `useEditorStore` holds it until the permission card's Open diff opens the read-only overlay; the permission prompt stays the only thing that decides, and closing the overlay decides nothing. `openFile`, a `showDiff` without `approvalId`, `openUntitled`, `showMarkdownPreview` and `openSettingsFile` open editor pane tabs. Only a user action takes focus: an agent's open passes `preserveFocus`, which shows its tab without focus, and VS Code maps the flag to `showTextDocument({ preserveFocus })`. Core closes an approval's `DiffView` on approve, reject, abort, an approval a mode change settles, and panel dispose (`approval-manager.ts`, `DiffManager.dispose`), which posts `editorCloseView`.
  - **Documents are main's, by id** (`documents/document-service.ts`). Renderers hold only main-issued document and tab ids. A shell open names a project key and a relative path; a core open may name any path, and a file outside every project opens read-only.
  - **Confinement** (`documents/confine.ts`): a path must be relative (`isRelativeFilePath`, plus `hasUnsafeWindowsSegment` on win32), and its native realpath must lie under the project's realpath, compared with `folderKey`. Before anything follows a link, `followsLocalLinksOnly` walks the path with one `lstat` per component and a `readlink` per link, and on win32 refuses as `outside` a link whose target is UNC, a device path (`\\?\`, `\\.\`, any `\??\` path but a drive) or drive-relative, or resolves onto a UNC or device root other than the project's. No realpath, stat or open reaches a share, because opening one sends the user's NTLM credentials; `confineExisting`, `confineEntry` and the Files listing's link kinds share the walk. Rename, trash and reveal act on a link itself, never its target. Save As confines the chosen path. A write compares the opened handle's bigint dev and ino with the identity taken before the realpath check, and a new file is created exclusively. Every later read of an open project document (a watcher reload, Revert, the disk check of a save or Overwrite, Compare) confines its path again first and is refused when it now resolves to another file (`DocumentService.readLocation`), and a write's link walk runs before its identity stat. A mention resolves only through `Platform.confinement` (`src/platform/file-confinement.ts`): desktop's is `confineExisting`; VS Code's follows links by realpath, since VS Code's own file service follows every workspace link and no VS Code view names a project file for a mention.
  - **Encoding:** a read records the encoding (UTF-8, UTF-16LE, UTF-16BE), the BOM and the majority EOL, and a save writes in place with all three. Text that is not valid UTF-8 without a BOM opens read-only. The size limit counts the bytes a write produces, BOM included (`encodedLength`), the same bytes `readFileBytes` measures, so a file saved at the limit reopens.
  - **Conflicts:** each document carries `{ mtimeMs, size, sha256 }`. A save over a changed file is refused and offers Compare, Overwrite (after a confirmation) or Revert. A watcher event reloads a clean buffer and flags a dirty one, so no text is discarded without the user choosing. The bar names the file on the file's own tab and on the Compare tab (`conflictCompare`), which offers only Overwrite and Revert; either, from either tab, closes the Compare tabs and shows the file's own tab with focus, opening one if none is left (`EditorPane.showResolved`, VS Code's `textFileSaveErrorHandler.ts`). A Compare tab's title follows its file's current name.
  - **Saves** (VS Code's `textFileEditorModel.ts` `doSave`, `saveSequentializer`): one document's saves, Save As, overwrites and reverts run one at a time in main (`DocumentService.inTurn`), so none reads the disk while another writes it. The shell runs one save per document with at most one queued, which a later request joins and which is skipped when nothing changed since the last save (`TaskSequentializer`). A save's text joins the buffer in the order it arrived among the shell's edits. A buffer that changed while the write ran stays dirty with the new text and its backup is scheduled; `diskText` is the text written. A write takes every byte before it truncates. A file that cannot be read before a save (a folder now, locked, past the limit) answers with the reason, which the tab shows. Every save, Save As and overwrite failure reads "Failed to save '{name}': {reason}" (VS Code's `textFileSaveErrorHandler.ts` genericSaveError). The reason comes from the errno code (`DocumentService.errnoReason`), or, for a write Damocles refuses itself (the file replaced during the save, a path that now leads out of the project, a disk that takes no more bytes), from the `WriteRefused` it throws; Search's replace in a closed file words its failure the same way. The raw error and path are only logged. A failed write sets `saveFailed` (VS Code's `handleSaveError`): the document is dirty and its backup is written at once, since a write that fails after some bytes landed may have torn the file, so the watcher flags the file as a conflict instead of loading it. Only a save that lands, Revert, or the disk found holding the buffer's text clears it; editing the text back does not, unlike VS Code, because the disk is no longer known to hold it. The shell turns a rejected save invoke into a failed save with a fixed reason under the tab (`saveNow`), so its save queue settles. A buffer with no file is never in conflict. A settings file saves against the sha256 of its bytes on disk. Auto save on focus change fires when the editor control blurs and when focus leaves the pane, once per document; Ctrl+S, Format Document and auto save act on an editable diff tab's modified side.
  - **Deleted files** (VS Code's orphaned model, `textFileEditorModel.ts` `setOrphaned`): a file deleted on disk leaves its document `orphaned`, which is not dirty; the buffer keeps its text and is dirty only when it differs from the last disk text. Its tab (`ShellEditorTab.deleted`) shows the name struck through in `--d-danger-text`, with Deleted in its tooltip and its accessible name. Save writes the file back, and the mark clears once the file exists again. Revert keeps the text and makes the buffer clean while the file stays gone (VS Code's `revert` ignores FILE_NOT_FOUND). Closing a clean orphaned tab and quitting ask nothing, and a clean orphaned document writes no backup. A hot-exit backup whose file is gone restores the same way, dirty, at the path the file had (`DocumentService.openProjectFile` with `missing`, confined through its deepest existing folder by `confineMissing`, VS Code's `doResolveFromBackup`). A save recreates the file and any folder deleted with it (`confineOrCreateFolder`, each folder confined again after its mkdir, as `fileService.ts` writeFile creates parent folders).
  - **A Files rename retargets open documents** (`DocumentService.rename`, `EditorPane.fileRenamed`, VS Code's `workingCopyFileService.ts` move and `editorService.ts` `handleMovedFile`). The rename on disk runs only once no file open is in flight, and a file open that starts meanwhile waits until the documents moved, so an open in flight never makes a second document at the old path, which the watcher would mark Deleted, and an open of the new name finds the moved one. Two opens of one file share one read (`textFileEditorModelManager.ts` `joinPendingResolves`). Every document at or under the old path keeps its id under the new path, confined again, with the new name and language, so its tabs keep their order and active state, the shell moves the document's Monaco model to a new one at the new path (`ShellEditorState.documents`, `editor-store.ts` `moveModel`, VS Code's `textFileEditorModelManager.ts`), carrying the unsaved text, EOL, indentation and each editor's view state; undo starts afresh there, as VS Code keeps undo per URI, and the old model is disposed. A model URI is `damocles-document://<documentId><path>`; a document with no file keeps the URI it was created with. A settings tab's model is at `inmemory://damocles/settings/<scope>/<documentId>.json`, which the settings schemas match by glob. The markdown preview renders the document's buffer when one exists, at most every 300 ms (VS Code's `preview.ts`), else main's content. Each tab's editor view state lives in the store by tab id until the tab closes (VS Code's editor memento), so a code or diff tab keeps its caret and scroll while another kind of tab shows. JSON follows VS Code's languages: plain `.json` flags comments and trailing commas, VS Code's JSON with Comments file names allow both (`JSONC_FILES` in `editor/monaco.ts`), and `.damocles/settings.json` and `.damocles/mcp.json` stay strict because Damocles parses them with `JSON.parse`. A diff tab names the file it shows (`DiffRequest.filePath`, titled by `title(fileName)`, VS Code's `diffEditorInput.ts`): a side's document when one is that file, else the file's place, which `fileRenamed` moves, so its title and path follow the rename while a snapshot side keeps its text. A dirty buffer's backup is rewritten at once under the new path, and the window layout and recent files name the new path; focus does not move. The location changes before any await, so a watcher read of the old path in flight is ignored. A settings file's document keeps its scope's path.
  - **The Files name box** (`FilesSection.vue`, `preload/file-names.ts`, VS Code's `explorerViewer.ts` and `validateFileName`): the name is checked against the loaded listing as it is typed (empty, invalid by `isValidFileName`, or a sibling's, ignoring case on Windows and macOS, the renamed entry's own name excluded). Enter does nothing while it is invalid, Escape cancels, and leaving the box commits a valid name and cancels an invalid one; only Enter and Escape move focus. Main checks again, and a refusal closes the box and shows one toast (`runFilesAction`).
  - **Files failures** (`index.ts` `runFilesAction`, `files/files-failure.ts`, VS Code's `fileActions.ts` deleteFiles): every failed create, rename, delete, copy path, reveal or folder expand (a click, Enter, Space or Right Arrow on a folder whose listing fails, which also collapses it), a throw included, is one error toast naming the entry and the reason; a cancel is silent. A New file or New folder into a folder whose listing fails opens no name box, and the listing's failure is that one toast. A listing reloaded after a file change fails silently. When the trash fails, the overlay's danger dialog offers Delete Permanently with the trash's reason; it deletes only the entry the user confirmed (bigint dev and ino compared again after the questions), a link as itself, and the tree reloads the folder after every outcome but a cancel.
  - **Hot exit** (`documents/backups.ts`): a dirty buffer backs up to `<userData>/backups/` 1 s after its last change, through a temp file renamed into place, and quit first flushes the shell's last edits (`editor:flush`, 2 s). With "Reopen where I left off" on, backups restore as dirty tabs; off, quit asks Save or Don't Save, and Escape cancels the quit. Every backup's text comes back (`EditorPane.restoreBackup`, VS Code's `workingCopyBackupTracker.ts` restoreBackups): into its file's document, a file deleted on disk included, else (its project removed, the file now outside it, unreadable, binary, too large or not UTF-8, a settings file that does not apply now, a second backup of a file another restored first) as an untitled buffer that keeps the backup, with one warning naming the file and why. No path removes a backup whose text was neither restored nor saved. A save of a dirty buffer writes its backup at once and waits for it to land before it writes the file in place, so a write a crash tears leaves the text in the backup; a write that fails backs the buffer up at once too, clean or not. A backup rename Windows refuses (EPERM, EBUSY, EACCES) is retried for a bounded time with `writeJsonConfig`'s `renameWithRetry`. A Revert that cannot take the file's text (too large, unreadable, binary, moved during the read) keeps the buffer, its conflict and its backup, and the tab says why.
  - **`readText`** returns the disk text on desktop, so a proposal diff's original side equals what the agent's tools read; VS Code returns the editor's text.
