- **Format on save runs the project's own Prettier in a formatter host, only in a trusted project** (`src/desktop/main/formatting/`, `src/desktop/formatter-host/`). With `damocles.desktop.editor.formatOnSave` on, a user save (Ctrl+S, Save As, the close and quit prompts, auto save on focus change) asks main to format the document by id first; auto save after a delay and agent writes never format. The result is applied as one undoable edit and dropped when the buffer changed meanwhile. Main resolves Prettier with Node resolution from the file's folder and uses it only when its package and entry lie inside the project root. It then finds the config file Prettier of that major would load, by that version's search order and rules (`prettier-resolve.ts`), and refuses one outside the root. The host loads exactly that file (`resolveConfig(file, { config, editorconfig: true, useCache: false })`, then `getFileInfo` with `resolveConfig: false` and the config's plugins); with none, it names Prettier's own `package.json`, which loads as no config, so Prettier never searches past the root. Config results are not cached, since nothing restarts the host when a config file changes. A `module.registerHooks` resolve hook in the host (`confine-modules.ts`, one synchronous hook for `require` and `import`) refuses every module whose real path is outside the root, builtins aside, so a plugin or shared config in a parent folder, a global folder or `NODE_PATH` never runs, and the notice names the path. `.editorconfig` files above the root are still read, as data. Prettier runs in `dist/formatter-host.js`, an Electron `utilityProcess` per root with the login environment minus `ELECTRON_*`, and no `MessagePort` reaches a renderer. A request has 3 s to load Prettier, its config, plugins and parser (the host's `loaded`) and then 3 s to format. Past the first, the save goes on unformatted while the host keeps loading (it is killed after 30 s, `HOST_STARTUP_MS`); past the second, main kills the host; either notice offers the Format log. Main also kills it on project removal and after 5 minutes idle, and restarts it when `package.json` or the lockfile changes. An untrusted project starts no host, and its first save in a run shows one notice with Trust. Without Prettier, TypeScript, JavaScript and JSON format with Monaco's built-in formatter in the shell. Prettier is never bundled.
- **Search runs ripgrep from main, and the renderer gets matches only** (`src/desktop/main/search/`). `rg-args.ts` builds an argv array: `--json --no-config --crlf --hidden`, never `--follow`, a regex as the value of `--regexp` with `--engine auto` and a fixed string after `--`, so a pattern starting with `-` is never an option. Globs are comma-separated and refused with a `..` segment or an absolute path; the shown folder is the only path, and `damocles.desktop.files.exclude` plus `damocles.desktop.search.exclude` add `-g !` entries unless the user turns exclude settings off. Main converts ripgrep's UTF-8 byte offsets to UTF-16 columns, bounds previews, and keeps only copies of the text it cuts from a ripgrep line (`detached`), because `--json` prints each matching line whole (`--max-columns` has no effect there) and a V8 substring keeps its whole parent alive. It kills ripgrep at `MAX_SEARCH_RESULTS` (20,000), on a new query, a project switch or Clear; one search runs per owner (the Search view, each Search Editor), 300 ms after the last keystroke. ripgrep searches files of any size, as VS Code does; main's memory is bounded by dropping any JSON event over `MAX_RG_EVENT_BYTES` (32 MiB), which marks the results a subset, and by keeping a match's text whole only up to `MAX_KEPT_MATCH_CHARS` and past it as its length and SHA-256, which replace verifies against (`keepMatchText`). Whole Word is ripgrep -w's: no Unicode word character on either side. Results carry main's smart-case `matchCase`, which the shell's buffer replace and row preview use; a file found closed and opened since is replaced only where its recorded text still is. A Search Editor run is dropped only when a searched field changed, and its running state clears either way; closing its tab or the window stops its ripgrep. Main searches the project's open documents and untitled tabs in their buffers, under one `runWithin` per run, and a buffer's matches replace ripgrep's for that file. `matchingPattern` makes every line break a regex names also match `\r\n` (VS Code's `fixRegexNewline`, lookbehinds included, which needs the bundled PCRE2 10.43 or later), and a multi-line regex scans a buffer's lines joined with `\n`, as Monaco does; "Search only in Open Editors" searches those buffers alone. Main owns the results and their dismissals, and an edit to an open document searches that file again 250 ms later, so a dismissed match stays dismissed until Refresh. Replace expands `$1` to `$99`, `$<name>`, `$&`, `$0`, `` $` ``, `$'` and `$$`, VS Code's `\n`, `\t` and `\\`, and its `\u`, `\l`, `\U` and `\L` before a numbered group, and applies Preserve Case, in one place (`replacementAt` in `src/shared/text-search.ts`): an open file changes in its buffer as one undoable edit, and a closed file is written through the document service only when every match's recorded text is still at its range, else it is skipped and listed, as is a file the service opens read-only. Overwrite after a save conflict sizes `savedText`, the text it writes, as Save and Save As do. A JavaScript RegExp built from user input runs under `runWithin` (`time-bound.ts`), which interrupts catastrophic backtracking. Replace All asks through `createMessageAsker`, and replaces run one at a time.
- **Quick Open lists and scores in a worker thread; main runs its ripgreps and relays** (`src/desktop/quick-open-worker/`, `src/desktop/main/quick-open.ts`, VS Code's `rawSearchService.ts`). `dist/quick-open-worker.js` is plain Node (no electron import, enforced by eslint), started on the first query from `app.asar.unpacked`. For each project it lists, the worker asks main, which runs `rg --files --hidden --no-config` (never `--follow`, excludes as `-g !`, the root after `--`) and streams its output in; the worker reads the paths, keeps every file sorted by path, and caches each list until main invalidates it (a create or delete under the project, a project or exclude setting change). There is no file cap. A query that extends an earlier one of the same open request scores only that one's matches, unless it adds a path separator or the earlier one was quoted; a failed listing is retried and its matches are never reused. A newer generation of the same request stops the older query between batches of 10,000 files. Main checks each reply as untrusted (at most 30 results, relative paths, ranges within the name, each file in a queried project and the scoped folder), names results from its own project list, and accepts a pick only if some query of the open request returned it. The worker posts progress at most once a second while ripgrep prints files and between scoring batches, and a worker that posts nothing for `QUICK_OPEN_WORKER_SILENCE_MS` (30 s, in main's timer ticks) while a query waits is stuck; a listing that still prints is never cut short, and a new query never extends the deadline. A worker error, exit, unreadable or malformed reply, failed query or silence answers every waiting query empty, is logged, terminates the worker and kills every ripgrep main runs for it, and the next query starts a fresh one. Main holds each ripgrep because terminating a worker thread leaves the processes it spawned running; ripgrep starts no process of its own (its Windows console host exits with it), so `ChildProcess.kill` through the handle is the whole kill, with no pid to reuse. VS Code's search process bounds a query only by cancellation.
- **A Search Editor is a document main owns, in VS Code's `.code-search` format** (`src/desktop/main/search/search-editor-format.ts`, `src/desktop/shell/editor/SearchEditorTab.vue`). Its text is the results body plus a header holding the query, toggles, globs and context lines, saved byte for byte as VS Code saves it, so a file opens in either app; dirty state and hot exit go through the document service. Results come only from main's search service. Parsing a `.code-search` file is bounded and never runs the query it holds; opening a result line reads main's own copy of the results and opens only a path inside the tab's project.
- **The command registry is the only command list** (`src/desktop/main/commands.ts`, plan AD11). The menu bar (`menu.ts`) and the palette are built from it; an app-level shortcut exists only as a menu item's accelerator, and native roles stay roles. Keys local to the focused widget stay in that widget: the terminal's find, copy, paste and context-menu keys (`terminal-keys.ts`) and Monaco's own bindings. Command ids are `MENU_IDS`. The palette's list and run channels take the focus context main captured when the palette opened; main refuses an unknown or disabled id, returns focus to the webContents that had it, then runs the command. A chat command reaches the selected chat only as `runChatCommand` after that view's `ready`, and the webview has one consumer for the union. A menu click runs an enabled command; an accelerator runs it only when `keyFires` holds.
- **The editor's context menu is the overlay's, never Monaco's** (`src/desktop/shell/editor/context-menu.ts`). Every shell Monaco editor is created with `contextmenu: false`; a right click on the text, the context menu key or Shift+F10 sends an overlay `menu` request with VS Code's editor menu as far as the page's language services fill it: Go to Definition and Go to References where `hasGoToProvider` (`editor/monaco.ts`) says the language has the provider, Rename Symbol and Change All Occurrences while their precondition holds, Format Document on a writable code, untitled or settings tab, Cut, Copy and Paste (Copy only in a read-only buffer) and Command Palette. Format Document runs the registry's format (D29) and Command Palette opens main's palette (`showCommands`). Cut, Copy and Paste are items with a `clipboard` kind that main runs itself on the user's pick, on the shell page while it holds keyboard focus. Main keeps them only in a menu opened by a context-menu click or key it observed (a one-shot grant in `askShellMenu`) and gives them its own label; the shell's `clipboard-read` permission stays denied. The shortcut labels come from `EDITOR_MENU_ACCELERATORS` in `commands.ts`, which must match Monaco's bindings for its commands.
- **Only the desktop chat panel page and the shell page allow workers, and only from `app://`.** Monaco's editor, JSON and TypeScript workers are module workers, and a worker cannot carry a nonce, so `script-src` does not cover it. The worker itself runs under the policy on its script response (`default-src 'none'; script-src app://damocles`), not the page's. The chat panel's workers come from `dist/webview/assets/monaco-*`: `PanelHost.workerSrc` is the one extension point, core appends `worker-src <value>;` to the chat panel CSP only when the host sets it, and the desktop chat sets `app://damocles/webview/assets/` (`CHAT_WORKER_SRC` in `src/desktop/main/views.ts`). The shell page sets `worker-src` to its own assets folder (`SHELL_WORKER_SRC` in `shell.ts`), with `base-uri` and `form-action 'none'`. No page adds `blob:`, a `font-src` or a `style-src` for them. `MonacoEnvironment.getWorker` (`components/editor/useMonaco.ts`) takes precedence over a language service's own worker, so every service label a page starts is registered there (the shell adds `typescript` and `javascript` through `addLanguageWorker`); an unregistered label gets the plain editor worker, and every call into that service fails with "Missing requestHandler or method". The overlay page, a browser page and every VS Code host set no `worker-src`, and `chat-panel-csp.test.ts` pins the VS Code chat panel and sidebar CSP byte for byte.
- **A settings file saves only text that the settings reader parses, and never overwrites a file it cannot parse** (`src/core/chat-panel/settings-file-editor.ts`). Desktop edits the three files `SettingsStore.scopeFile` names; project and local only for a trusted default project (`defaultProjectPath`, the store's own rule), checked again at save. `parseSettingsText` (`src/core/config/settings-file.ts`) is the single parser the desktop store, the editor, the permission rule writer (`syncPermissionRulesToSettings`) and the permission rule reader (`readPermissionsFromPath`, for all eight settings files, `.claude` included) share; it ignores a leading BOM, so a rule the store applies is never one the evaluator drops. The permission rule writer refuses a file that does not parse the same way, inside the lock, and the user is told the rule was not saved. Desktop opens each file as a settings tab in the editor pane (`DocumentService.openSettingsFile`), and `DocumentService.saveSettings` saves it through `saveSettingsFileText`, which runs inside `writeJsonConfig`'s lock and refuses, in order: a file on disk that does not parse, a file whose sha256 no longer equals the version the tab loaded (a conflict), and text that does not parse (the parser's message shows under the tab); otherwise it writes the user's text verbatim. A conflict goes to the editor's conflict bar (Compare, Overwrite after a confirmation, Revert) like any file's, and nothing discards the text without the user choosing so. A save is refused, with no conflict, when the default project moved after the tab opened. A path that exists but cannot be read (a directory, no permission) is answered with `unavailable`/`unreadable`, so nothing waits on it. The document service watches the file and reloads a clean tab or marks a dirty one in conflict, never for its own save. The settings modal's footer menu (Edit settings.json) learns which files apply from the `settingsFileAvailability` that `requestSettingsState` posts, which reads no file, and the host posts it again to each panel that asked on a trust grant or a project change. On desktop that footer closes the modal and sends `openSettingsFileInChat`, which opens or shows that scope's settings tab in the editor pane (`platform.editor.openSettingsFile`). The editor's schema (`src/shared/generated/settings-schema.json`) is generated from `package.json` plus `permissions` by `scripts/generate-settings-schema.mjs`: unknown keys are flagged, and in the project variant every user-only key is flagged with a message.
- **A directory that appears under a desktop recursive watch is watched before it is listed, and what it holds is reported as created** (`src/desktop/main/platform/file-watcher-factory.ts`, `src/desktop/watch-worker/fs-watch-tree.ts`). On Linux the recursive watch is `startInotifyTree`, one non-recursive `fs.watch` per directory, never `@parcel/watcher`'s inotify backend: that backend adds a watch to a new directory without listing it, so a directory created with its contents in one go, or moved in, loses those files, and its subdirectories are never watched (parcel-bundler/watcher#97, #243). inotify(7) prescribes the order the tree follows: watch the new directory, then list it. The tree reports a change only against what it already reported, so a file both the listing and an event see is created once. On macOS parcel's native recursive watch sees later writes, but a directory moved in still arrives as a single create, so `startParcel` lists it and reports what the batch did not. On Windows the recursive `fs.watch` already covers a new directory, and `startFsWatchTree` lists it and reports what its snapshot did not hold. All three skip `RECURSIVE_IGNORE`. The startup scans are asynchronous (Linux's stats directories only), so a large workspace never blocks the main process.
- **Windows watches recursively with Node's `fs.watch(root, { recursive: true })`, one handle per root, and nothing on Windows loads `@parcel/watcher`, in the app or in tests** (`startFsWatchTree`; `electron-builder.yml` `files` leaves its Windows prebuild out of every package, and its JS loader stays unused). Exclusions stay in the top-level `files`: electron-builder takes a platform `files` set that only excludes as everything but those paths, and ships the whole project. parcel 2.6.0's Windows backend frees a subscription on a libuv worker while its `ReadDirectoryChangesW` read is still pending (parcel-bundler/watcher#262), which crashed the main process on quit and on unwatch (0xC0000005), whereas libuv closes the watch on the loop thread and frees it only once that read has completed. The trees run on one watch worker thread per factory (`src/desktop/watch-worker/`, `platform/watch-worker-host.ts`), because libuv's Windows watch makes a synchronous `GetLongPathNameW` call on the loop that owns it for every created or modified entry, before any JS filter runs: with `npm ci` in a watched 25,000-file tree, main-thread stalls over 50 ms were 1-2 with no watcher, 241-292 (up to 1.25 s) with `fs.watch` on main, and 1 with it on a worker. A worker that exits, errors, sends a reply main cannot read or check, or answers `closed` unasked is terminated, and the host at once starts a fresh one and posts it every live watch with `report: false`, logging one line with the count, as VS Code's watcher restart watches its requests again without replaying them. The snapshot each watch was diffed against died with the worker, so the fresh worker's first scan only records what it finds: nothing is reported as created or deleted that did not change, and a change made between the failure and that scan is not reported. A root gone by then fails to watch and is waited for again, without its deletes. Restarts stop after `MAX_WORKER_RESTARTS` (5, VS Code's limit): the host then logs once and every Windows recursive watch fires nothing until the app restarts, so a worker that dies on every start cannot respawn forever.
- **The Windows tree settles every event against a snapshot of every entry, and diffs the tree again when events were lost** (`startFsWatchTree`). Node reports a create, a delete and both names of a rename as `rename` and a last-access update as `change`, so each path, coalesced for 75 ms, is `lstat`ed and compared with its recorded identity (file id, size, ctime): an unchanged file reports nothing, so a read never looks like a write. Files are recorded as well as directories, at about 250 bytes per entry, because a directory's delete reports its children and the diff below needs them. A burst past libuv's 4 KB change buffer arrives as a null filename: the root is listed again and diffed against the snapshot, one scan at a time with one more queued and logged at most once a minute, while events wait until it ends. An `error` closes the handle, and a root still there is watched again after 1 s, doubling to a minute while that keeps failing, then diffed. A same-size rewrite inside the file system's timestamp tick after the snapshot read the file goes unreported, as with VS Code's mtime-and-size etag.
- **`FileWatcherFactory.onDidRenameFiles` never fires on desktop.** No rename is reported, the Files section's own included: a rename reaches watchers as a delete plus a create, exactly as VS Code reports a rename made outside its explorer. The editor follows a Files rename itself (`EditorPane.fileMoved`).
- **A watcher glob is relative, on both hosts and in the fake** (`src/platform/file-watcher.ts`): `watch(base, glob)` matches paths relative to `base`, and `watchWorkspace(glob)` matches paths relative to each open folder and follows folders being added and removed. VS Code gets one `RelativePattern` per workspace folder, because a string glob there is matched against the absolute path and a glob without a leading `**/` would never fire. The fake platform's `fire*` reaches listeners only for a path the glob matches, so a core test cannot pass with a glob that never fires.
- **Desktop watchers never throw and never die quietly** (`platform/file-watcher-factory.ts`). `watch()` logs a watch it cannot start and returns a watcher that fires nothing; every listener runs even when another throws (`platform/emitter.ts`, as VS Code's `Emitter`), which the settings store, the key-value state and localization use too. A watched root that is deleted (a `git clean` removing `.damocles`) reports its files deleted, closes its watch and waits on its nearest ancestor again, and a recreated root replays what it holds; on Windows the closed watch also ends the endless rename events Node reports for a deleted watched directory. A recursive root is watched at its real path, because FSEvents reports real paths (`/var` is `/private/var`), and events map back onto the path the caller gave. A non-recursive root reports a burst of raw events once per file after 75 ms, as VS Code's non-recursive watcher does. Every `@parcel/watcher` subscribe and unsubscribe (macOS) and every Windows tree scan goes through its host's `track`, a dispose before its subscribe lands unsubscribes once it does, and the quit awaits `DesktopFileWatcherFactory.close()`, which also waits for the watch worker's scans to settle before it ends the worker, after which no watch starts native work and no scan emits: a native completion while Node frees its environment aborts the process (`napi_throw` in `PromiseRunner::onWorkComplete`).

## Desktop packaging and updates

- **The packaged layout comes from `app.isPackaged`, never from a path guess** (`src/desktop/main/platform/app-paths.ts`, `app-info.ts`). Packaged, `resourceRoot` is `app.getAppPath()` (`resources/app.asar`), `unpackedRoot` is its `app.asar.unpacked` sibling, the three worker entries, the Quick Open worker (`quickOpenWorkerPath`) and the watch worker (`watchWorkerPath`) resolve under `unpackedRoot/dist`, and `AppInfo.version` is `app.getVersion()`. Unpackaged (`electron dist/desktop/main.js` from a checkout), all three roots are the repo root and the version comes from the repo `package.json`, because `app.getVersion()` then reports Electron's version. `package.json` `main` stays the VS Code entry; electron-builder gives the app its own with `extraMetadata.main: dist/desktop/main.js`. electron-updater reads the same version (`app.getVersion()`) and its feed from `process.resourcesPath`, the directory that holds `app.asar`.
- **`asarUnpack` has one source: `ASAR_UNPACK` in `src/desktop/main/platform/unpacked-assets.ts`.** `electron-builder.yml` `asarUnpack` must equal it, and `app-paths.test.ts` fails when they drift. A file belongs there when something other than the main process's asar-patched `fs` reads it as a real file: the worker scripts (worker threads and the sentinel, which runs as Node in a separate process), the tree-sitter grammars and `web-tree-sitter` (read from the unpacked compass worker), the Python sidecar package (Python cannot import from an archive), ripgrep (`spawn` cannot execute a file inside an asar), the formatter host and the pty host (`dist/formatter-host.js`, `dist/pty-host.js`, which a `utilityProcess` loads as a file), the shell-integration scripts (`resources/shell-integration/**`, which a shell reads), node-pty's target prebuild (`node_modules/node-pty/prebuilds/**`; node-pty's JS stays in `app.asar`, because node-pty rewrites its `spawn-helper` path from `app.asar` to `app.asar.unpacked`) and the native Node-API prebuilds of koffi and `@parcel/watcher`. The Windows package holds no `@parcel/watcher` prebuild, so its glob (`WATCHER_UNPACK`) matches nothing there, and the package check takes each platform's globs from `asarUnpackFor`. A new consumer that hands a bundled file to a worker, a child process or another runtime adds its path to `ASAR_UNPACK`; it never relies on reading that file through the archive. The one exception is node-pty's conout worker on Windows (`lib/worker/conoutSocketWorker.js`), which node-pty starts from `app.asar` because its path rewrite handles only `node_modules.asar`; Electron's worker threads read the archive, and the packaged e2e run proves a shell starts. `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs` checks the unpacked tree of every CI build.
- **Fuses are set by electron-builder and read back from every built binary** (`electron-builder.yml` `electronFuses`, `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs`, which reads the fuse wire with `@electron/fuses` `getCurrentFuseWire` on each CI leg). `runAsNode` is enabled; `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable`, `enableNodeCliInspectArguments` and `grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` are disabled; `enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation`, `onlyLoadAppFromAsar` and `enableCookieEncryption` are enabled. Never weaken a fuse to make a test pass and never add an app-side environment hook instead: with `--inspect` disabled, Playwright's `_electron.launch` cannot attach to a packaged app, so tests against the packaged app drive its renderers through `--remote-debugging-port`, which reaches renderers only.
  - **`runAsNode` stays enabled because the POSIX shell sentinel needs it.** `startShellSentinel` spawns `process.execPath` with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, and the sentinel must outlive a crash of the main process, which a `utilityProcess` does not. Residual risk, accepted: a process already running as the user can use the Damocles binary as a Node interpreter and inherit the app's OS permissions, including its macOS privacy (TCC) grants. VS Code keeps the fuse enabled for the same reason.
  - **With `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` off, the packaged app ignores an inherited `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`.** Electron strips it together with `NODE_OPTIONS` at startup, so `installCaCertificates` trusts the bundled roots and the OS store only. On macOS and Linux the file is still added when the login shell environment merge brings the variable back (it is set in the user's shell profile); on Windows, where there is no merge, it is never read. Corporate CAs belong in the OS certificate store. The VS Code extension and the unpackaged dev app are unaffected. Do not turn the fuse on to restore the variable: it would reopen `NODE_OPTIONS` injection.
  - **Files under `app.asar.unpacked` are outside the asar integrity check.** Electron validates asar integrity on macOS and Windows only, so on Linux `app.asar` itself is unchecked too and the root-owned `/opt/Damocles` is the whole protection. Where it runs, integrity validation covers `app.asar` only, so the unpacked worker scripts (which run inside the app), `sentinel.js`, the formatter and pty hosts (`dist/formatter-host.js`, `dist/pty-host.js`), the Quick Open and watch workers, the shell-integration scripts (`resources/shell-integration/**`, which every integrated shell sources), the Python package, the grammars and the native modules are protected only by the install directory's file permissions: root-owned under `/opt/Damocles` on Linux, the user's own `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs` on a per-user Windows install, and `/Applications` on macOS.
- **Linux never runs without the Chromium sandbox.** Nothing launches the Damocles binary or its Chromium processes with `--no-sandbox` (not the app, its scripts, CI or the docs), and there is no AppImage, whose electron-builder workaround is exactly that flag. Ubuntu 24.04 and later restrict unprivileged user namespaces, so the deb and rpm `afterInstall` script (`build/linux/after-install.sh`) installs `/etc/apparmor.d/damocles`, an unconfined profile that grants `userns` to `/opt/Damocles/damocles` only, and reloads that one profile. It installs nothing when AppArmor is absent or older than 4.0, and it never fails the install on an AppArmor error. `after-remove.sh` removes the profile only on a real removal, never on an upgrade. The script makes `chrome-sandbox` setuid instead on kernels without unprivileged user namespaces, and when AppArmor restricts them (`kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`) but the profile did not load, so Chromium falls back to its setuid sandbox rather than failing to start. Residual, accepted: AppArmor keeps an unconfined profile's label across exec of a binary with no profile of its own, so every descendant (agent shell commands, `git`, MCP servers, the voice sidecar) may create user namespaces too. That is the posture Ubuntu ships for Chrome and VS Code. `build/linux/**` is LF through `.gitattributes`, because a CRLF shebang or profile fails on Linux.
- **The update model differs per OS, and the feed is only the packaged `resources/app-update.yml`** (`src/desktop/main/updater.ts`). The updater starts after the window is up, returns before it loads electron-updater, and is off in the unpackaged app. It never calls `setFeedURL`, never sets `forceDevUpdateConfig` or `updateConfigPath`, and reads no environment variable or argument; `updater.test.ts` guards all of these. `allowPrerelease` is off, so every platform reads the latest stable release.
  - Windows and Linux download in the background, then offer **Restart Now**; a dismissed update installs on quit (`autoInstallOnAppQuit`). Linux deb and rpm install through electron-updater's own `pkexec` path; the app adds no elevation of its own. Restart Now (the notice, the pill and About) runs the app's quit first, with its terminal and unsaved-editor questions, and calls electron-updater's `quitAndInstall` only after the quit tore everything down, as VS Code's `quitAndInstall` does; while that quit runs the state is `restarting`, which the pill, its menu and About show with Restart disabled, and main refuses a second restart; a cancelled quit returns the state to `ready`, and a later restart works.
  - Windows sets `autoUpdater.channel` to `latest-<arch>` because both architectures release from one tag and would otherwise both write `latest.yml`; electron-builder's combined multi-arch NSIS installer is not an option (non-deterministic `files` order since 26.0.13, issue 9745). `allowDowngrade = false` is set after the channel, because electron-updater's channel setter turns downgrades on. Feeds: `latest-x64.yml`, `latest-arm64.yml`, `latest-mac.yml`, `latest-linux.yml`, `latest-linux-arm64.yml`.
  - macOS cannot apply an update without a Developer ID signature (Squirrel.Mac checks the signature against the installed app), so `autoDownload` is off and an available update raises a notice whose one action opens `https://github.com/AizenvoltPrime/damocles/releases/tag/v<version>` through `ShellService.openExternal`. The version must match a plain release version pattern first, so a feed cannot turn the notice into another URL. Adding a Developer ID later is configuration only; see `docs/desktop-signing.md`.
  - `UpdateService` publishes one state (`disabled` in the unpackaged app, `idle`, `checking`, `upToDate`, `available` on macOS, `downloading`, `ready`, `restarting` while the quit that installs a ready update runs, `error`) to the title-bar pill and Settings › About. It checks after the window is up, every 4 hours, on a resume from sleep once the last check (`damocles.desktop.update.lastCheckedAt`, global state) is that old, and on demand from About and Help › Check for Updates…; recording `lastCheckedAt` happens in the background, so a failed write is logged and never stops the check or its download; a check while one or a download runs does nothing. Each state that needs the user posts one `NotificationService` notice. Help › About Damocles, Release Notes and Check for Updates… sit in the app menu on macOS, which has no `role: 'about'`.
  - What's new reads `dist/desktop/CHANGELOG.md`, which the desktop build copies and `verify-desktop-package.mjs` checks inside `app.asar` against the repo's file and the packaged version's section. `release-notes.ts` indexes the `## [x.y.z] - date` headings, skipping any other, and sends one version's markdown per request; a failed read is logged with its path and not cached, so the next request reads again, and the overlay shows a fixed localized reason, never main's error text; the overlay sanitizes it with DOMPurify and opens links through `openExternal`. The first launch of a higher version than `damocles.desktop.lastSeenVersion` posts one "Damocles {0} is installed" notice; a first install, the same version and a downgrade post none.
  - electron-updater both emits `error` and rejects with the same error. The updater handles both and logs each error once through the log sink, so an update failure can neither crash the main process nor raise an unhandled rejection. A failed check (offline, a captive portal, a firewall blocking GitHub) is only logged, because it would otherwise warn on every such launch; once an update was found, the first failure to download, install or offer it shows one warning per run.
  - Update tests point an installed test copy at a loopback feed by rewriting its `resources/app-update.yml` (`scripts/desktop-update-override.mjs`, driven by `e2e/desktop/update/drive-update.mjs`). That file sits outside `app.asar`, so the app needs no test hook; the writer accepts only an `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` generic feed and only over the packaged GitHub config.

## Tests

- Every test file runs under its own temp home: `src/__mocks__/hermetic-home.setup.ts` points `USERPROFILE` and `HOME` at a fresh dir before any module reads `os.homedir()`, so nothing a test writes through `DAMOCLES_HOME_DIR` reaches the developer's real `~/.damocles`. That setup file must never import `paths.ts`, which freezes the home at import. It also points `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` at a fresh dir, so `os.tmpdir()` sits under the same per-run root, which `test-home.global-setup.ts` removes after the fork workers exit. A test still removes what it creates: the run root only catches what a failed test leaves behind. Each root holds an `owner.pid` with the Vitest process's PID, and the next run's setup removes every root whose owner has exited, which is what an interrupted run (Ctrl+C, a killed terminal) leaves. A root without the file is never removed, because a concurrent run may have just created it.
- Those homes live under one per-run root that `src/__mocks__/test-home.global-setup.ts` removes in its teardown. Cleanup belongs there and not in a per-file `afterAll`: vitest skips a file's hooks when every test in it is skipped or filtered out by `-t`, and the teardown runs after the fork workers exit, when Windows has released their file handles.
- The redirect needs vitest's `forks` pool, pinned in `vitest.config.ts`. In a worker thread, `process.env` writes do not reach `os.homedir()`.
- **The OS clipboard is locked across e2e workers** (`e2e/desktop/support/clipboard.ts`). A test that touches the clipboard takes the `clipboard` fixture first, so the lock spans every app it launches, and reads and writes only through it. `scripts/__tests__/e2e-clipboard.test.ts` fails on any other clipboard access, OS clipboard tool, or copy and paste key, click or menu pick outside the fixture.
- **The OS foreground is locked across e2e workers** (`e2e/desktop/support/foreground.ts`). A test that asserts native focus or relies on hover takes the `foreground` fixture first: it waits until no other worker has an app open, and no worker opens one until it ends. The `clipboard` fixture holds the foreground too, since a paste needs keyboard focus, so a test takes one or the other, never both; `scripts/__tests__/e2e-foreground.test.ts` fails a test that reads native focus or relies on hover with neither, and one that takes both. Both locks serve their waiters in arrival order (`fair-lock.ts`): a waiter first takes a ticket in the OS temp folder, and only the oldest ticket whose worker is alive and refreshed it within 10 s tries the `proper-lockfile` lock, so a worker that releases a lock and asks again queues behind every worker already waiting. A plain lock retry lets the releasing worker win every time, so one running foreground tests back to back starved another worker's launch past its test timeout. A launch's wait for another worker's hold runs outside its test's time (`outsideTestTime` in `fixtures.ts`), as the two fixtures' waits run in their own fixture timeout; `scripts/__tests__/e2e-foreground-queue.test.ts` runs the queue in real worker processes.
- **A test that must act before a chat's page is ready holds it** (`e2e/desktop/support/hold-chat-pages.cjs`, `LaunchOptions.require`, dev launches only). The module, required ahead of the main script, holds every `app://damocles/panel/` load until `__e2eChatPages.release()` and counts each view's focus gains. Keys meant for main's input observers go through `pressKeys`, because Playwright's CDP key presses never reach `before-input-event`.
- **A desktop launch must quit with exit code 0** (`e2e/desktop/support/app.ts`). `DesktopApp.close()` waits for the main process to exit and rejects, once, when it died of a signal or exited with another code, as when a native module aborts in Node's environment teardown (`FATAL ERROR` in the main output); the fixture closes every launch, so a crash on the way out fails its test. A process killed by a signal keeps `exitCode` null, so an exit check reads `signalCode` too.
- **Desktop end-to-end launches stay off the developer's keyring** (`e2e/desktop/support/hermetic.ts`). Each launch gets a temp home and an allowlisted environment with `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS=disabled:`, because libsecret otherwise finds the session bus through `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/bus` and safeStorage reads and writes the real Linux keyring. `DAMOCLES_E2E_OS_KEYRING=1` opts in, and only then does `secrets.spec.ts` "encrypted at rest" run on Linux. On macOS every launch runs with `--use-mock-keychain`: Playwright's Electron loader appends it (with `--password-store=basic`) to each dev launch, and `support/packaged-app.ts` and `update/drive-update.mjs` pass the same two switches, because the real Keychain's modal prompt blocks the main thread on a runner and would add the app's Safe Storage item on a developer's Mac. Windows DPAPI stores nothing outside userData. Every launch's git sends GitHub URLs to a missing folder, so nothing is cloned, and `seedSubscriptionPlugin` installs a local stub of the pinned subscription plugin. A teardown that cannot delete the temporary home names the leftovers and, on Windows, the processes Restart Manager reports holding them (`support/file-holders.ts`).
- **Review screenshots are their own Playwright project, never part of the suite** (`e2e/desktop/screenshots/*.screenshots.ts`, `support/screenshots.ts`). `dev`, and so `npm run test:desktop` and the WSL script, matches only `*.spec.ts`; after the build `test:desktop` runs, the captures run with `npx playwright test --config playwright.desktop.config.ts --project=screenshots`. Each PNG goes to `DAMOCLES_SCREENSHOT_DIR` when it is set (Playwright never wipes it), else to the test's folder under `dist/e2e-results/screenshots`, and is attached to the HTML report. A capture waits for what it shows (the theme on the page's body, the emulated viewport, the element) and then for `settled` (fonts, two frames, every finite animation except the toast life bar, a countdown), never for a fixed time. The `proto-*` captures render the design reference from the untracked `Damocles desktop UI revamp/export/damocles-revamp` folder, which loads its scripts from the network; they are taken again on every run, and without the folder they are skipped with a `reference` annotation. A whole-window capture goes through `saveWindowScreenshot`, and a capture showing a browser page waits for `pageFramesShown`.
- **The desktop suite runs on Linux from Windows through WSL** (`node scripts/test-desktop-wsl.mjs --distro Ubuntu-24.04 [specs...]`), as the release workflow's Linux leg does: the script syncs the working tree into `~/damocles-e2e` in the distro, runs `npm ci` there only when `package-lock.json` changed, builds, and runs the `dev` project under Xvfb and openbox. The distro needs Node 24 through nvm, xvfb-run, openbox, xauth and rsync, and the script refuses the Windows npm the distro's PATH carries. Its rsync file list lives in the OS temp folder and is removed on exit, Ctrl+C included. Every step runs under the distro's `timeout --kill-after`, which signals the step's whole process group, because killing `wsl.exe` leaves Xvfb, Electron and Playwright running in the distro.

## Packaging

- **The VSIX node_modules allowlist is generated, never hand-edited** (`scripts/sync-vscodeignore.mjs`, run through `npm run sync:vscodeignore`). `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs the script with `--check` on `ubuntu-latest` while developers generate the block on their own machines, so every pattern it emits has to be identical on every platform. A native binary package therefore collapses to a family glob (`@vscode/ripgrep-win32-x64` becomes `@vscode/ripgrep-*`), and a name that is nothing but platform tokens collapses to its scope (`@esbuild/win32-x64` becomes `@esbuild/*`). A literal platform name in the block fails `--check` on the release runner and aborts the tag push before the package matrix runs. `scripts/__tests__/sync-vscodeignore.test.ts` pins both halves.
- **node-pty ships only the target's prebuild** (`electron-builder.yml`, `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs`). `node-pty` is pinned exactly at `1.2.0-beta.15`, the version VS Code pins, because stable 1.1.0 lacks Linux prebuilds and its macOS `spawn-helper` lacks the execute bit. The package keeps only `prebuilds/<platform>-<arch>/`, with no `*.pdb`, `build`, `deps`, `src`, `third_party` or `scripts`. The verifier asserts exactly one prebuild directory, matching the target, its native binaries unpacked, node-pty's JS inside `app.asar`, and on macOS `spawn-helper` at 0755 with `codesign -v` passing on it and on `pty.node`. The publish job's `npm ci --ignore-scripts` builds nothing, and node-pty's install script only checks that the prebuild exists.
- **Main confirms and confines every terminal link** (`src/desktop/main/terminal/terminal-links.ts`, `src/desktop/shell/terminal/terminal-links.ts`). The shell sends only candidate path text that the link parser (`terminal-link-parsing.ts`) finds, within VS Code's limits, and never reads the file system. Main refuses UNC, device, drive-relative, root-relative and `~` paths before any file system call, and in a WSL terminal any POSIX path outside `/mnt/<drive>/`. It resolves the rest against the terminal's base folder, requires the result inside the terminal's project, and confirms it with `confineExisting` and `stat`. Opening a link resolves it again. A file goes through `openFromUser`. A folder is revealed in Files only while Files shows its project; otherwise Quick Open opens limited to that folder of that project (`folderLinkAction`, `QuickOpenScope`) and leaves the selected chat and project as they are. Neither path takes an absolute path from a renderer.
- **Main parses shell integration, and the nonce decides trust** (`src/desktop/main/terminal/shell-integration.ts`). Main strips every OSC 633 sequence from a terminal's output before the renderer sees it, trusted or not, and the renderer registers no 633 handler. A sequence is trusted only when its field count is exact for its type and its last field equals the terminal's nonce (128 bits, new on every spawn and restart, passed only in the environment, unset by each script). Main alone owns a terminal's cwd, running command, title and command facts; link resolution, kill confirmation and every menu and dialog read only those, and the renderer gets validated events in-band with the output. Main acks stripped characters to the pty host itself. The nonce stops program output, not code running as the user, which can read it as in VS Code.
- **Shell-integration scripts load only from the app's `resources/shell-integration`** (`shell-integration-injection.ts`, unpacked when packaged). A script path travels as its own argv entry or in the environment, never inside shell source, and every escaper hex-escapes all control bytes. zsh's startup files are copied to `<userData>/shell-integration/zsh`. wsl.exe and cmd.exe get no injection, as in VS Code. The bash, zsh and fish scripts are LF (`.gitattributes`), and the package verifier checks them.
- **Every terminal kill goes through one confirmation** (`terminal/kill-confirmation.ts`). Kill, Kill All, a row's kill, closing the window, removing a project and quitting ask through `TerminalService` per `damocles.desktop.terminal.confirmOnKill`, in one D41 dialog that is the only wait. Command marks come only from main's events: a command without a reported command line gets no mark, and Rerun and Copy Command never read screen rows.
- **Terminal output reaches a prompt only as a framed attachment block** (`src/core/terminal-attachment.ts`). Each attachment is its own block before the IDE block and the typed text, says it is data, and is capped by `capTerminalText` in the renderer and again in main. A composer's pending attachments hold at most `MAX_PENDING_TERMINAL_ATTACHMENT_CHARS` (128,000) characters together; an Add to Chat past it is refused with a toast and the chips stay. A `damocles-terminal-attachments` sidecar entry records how many blocks lead a user entry. `PiSession.recordPromptSidecars` writes it together with `damocles-original-input` when pi commits the entry, for a prompt that opened a run and for one pi queued into a running run (`queuedPromptEntries`: matched by text at delivery, oldest first, withdrawn when a Stop, the budget, a new chat, a resume switch or the session's disposal drops pi's queue), and never for a prompt that committed nothing. Display readers (the history, list metadata and the rewind list) strip exactly that many, so typed text that imitates a block stays text. Memory queries, memory extraction and title generation drop the blocks. Attachments leave the composer only with a prompt that commits. `PiSession.sendMessage` returns `unsent` for one refused or stopped before its run, and the handler puts them back (`TerminalAttachmentManager.restore`), while `interruptRecovery` carries the typed text and images (`contentBlocks`) back to the composer after whatever it holds. One the session's disposal returns before pi queued it or opened a run with it, while pi's input handlers hold it included, goes back at once through `interruptRecovery` and `onWithdrawn`, and `sendMessage` then returns `withdrawn`, which the handler leaves alone as it does `sent`. For one pi queued into a running run it returns `sent`, and calls the handler's `onWithdrawn` when a Stop, the budget, a new chat, a resume switch or the session's disposal (a folder switch, a cross-folder resume) drops pi's queue before pi delivers it (`dropQueue`, `queuedPromptEntries.withdraw`). The same withdrawal posts `queueCancelled` with `returnToInput` under the prompt's correlation id, so the webview takes the echo out and puts its typed text and images back. A prompt pi queues, after its input handlers ran, into a run already stopped or a session already replaced is withdrawn at once from `preflightResult('queued')`. One a Stop withdrew while those handlers held it, which pi then queues into a newer live run, is withdrawn alone (`queuedPromptEntries.withdrawOne`) and taken back out of pi's queue (`takeBackQueued`). pi acts on a slash invocation only at the start of the prompt, so when blocks would lead one, `chat-handlers.ts` resolves it first (`slash-invocation.ts`, a port of pi's extension command check and its skill and template expansion, with a parity test against pi): a template or skill command goes expanded behind the blocks, and an extension command runs alone while the chips stay pending and a notice says so.
- **A terminal paste never hands the clipboard to a renderer, and happens only for a paste gesture main observed** (`src/desktop/main/terminal/terminal-paste.ts`, `clipboard-gestures.ts`). The shell stops xterm's native paste events in the capture phase. Main issues a one-shot grant, bound to the active terminal and to `clipboard` or `selection`, when it observes the paste: a `before-input-event` keyDown of `isTerminalPasteChord` (the shell's key handler uses the same predicate) while the xterm's input has focus, a Linux middle `mouseDown` in `before-mouse-event`, a pick of the terminal menu's Paste, or Edit › Paste while the xterm's input has focus. The pane reports whether focus is the xterm's input; anywhere else Edit › Paste does what the paste roles do. The grant lasts 2 s in main's timer ticks, clears when the window page loses focus, and answers one `damocles:terminal:paste` naming that terminal and source while it is active and focused; anything else is logged and reads nothing. Main reads PRIMARY only for a `selection` grant, applies the multi-line and control-character warning (D41), strips ESC inside a bracketed paste, and writes the text to the pty itself. Edit › Paste and macOS Paste and Match Style are menu items, not roles.
- **The terminal runs in one pty host process, and main issues every terminal** (`src/desktop/pty-host/`, `src/desktop/main/terminal/`). The pty host is an Electron `utilityProcess` (`dist/pty-host.js`) with the login environment minus `ELECTRON_*`, so a native crash or a blocked read cannot take down main; no `MessagePort` reaches a renderer. A terminal's environment (`terminal-env.ts`) is that login environment minus every variable the integration scripts read or export (`SCRIPT_STATE_ENV` in `shell-integration-injection.ts`) and minus a `ZDOTDIR` naming the app's own zsh folder, so a Damocles started from its own terminal starts clean shells; any other `DAMOCLES_` variable is the user's and passes. A missing `LANG`, or one not ending in `.UTF-8`, `.utf8` or `.euc…`, becomes `<locale>.UTF-8` from `app.getLocale()`, as VS Code's `terminal.integrated.detectLocale` `auto` does, because a macOS app opened from the Dock or Finder inherits none. Main drops any variable the host's parser refuses (`isHostEnvEntry`) or past `MAX_HOST_ENV_ENTRIES` before it posts a spawn, and logs its name, never its value. On Windows only spawns and kills wait for the conpty throttle, in order on their own chain, so a kill sent while its spawn waits finds the pty; input, resize, ack and clearAck run at once, or in order behind their own terminal's waiting spawn. A window close sends one `killAll` and a quit one `shutdown`, both unthrottled, each cancelling every spawn still waiting. node-pty runs a Windows pty's kill only on its first output, so `shutdown` ends the host only once every pty it started has printed or exited (`connecting` in `sessions.ts`), within main's 2 s bound; a host that exits sooner never closes that pseudoconsole, and its shell can outlive the app. A Windows shell that exits with CreateProcess error 193, 267 or 1260 before any output shows a localized line saying why, and a restart starts in the project folder once its launch folder is gone; input typed while a restart checks that folder is held in main and sent after the spawn. Main issues ids `term-<n>` and accepts a create only as a detected profile id plus a project key, resolving the cwd itself. The host batches output every 5 ms or 64 KB and applies VS Code's flow control: pause above 100,000 unacknowledged characters, resume below 5,000, acknowledge every 5,000. A host exit marks every terminal exited with its code and a Restart action. Main kills a project's terminals when the project is removed, a terminal on close, and every terminal at once on window close and quit. The window layout restores the terminal list at launch without taking focus: main reads the saved list when the window attaches, and `restore()` recreates it ahead of any terminal started before the window page loaded, which stays active. The xterm is fitted only while its screen is visible (`TerminalView.vue`), because xterm 6 resizes a paused renderer at idle time and meanwhile takes the clamped scroll for the user scrolling up, after which it stops following output; the terminal pane glides open from no height. The pty learns a size only once it has held for 100 ms (`pty-resize.ts`, VS Code's resize debouncer), so a pane gliding open resizes the shell once. Input and a paste send a waiting size first, so the shell reads the user's keys at the width on screen and readline's redraw of a prompt wrapped at the old width cannot erase the output above it. While an xterm's own input has focus (VS Code's `terminalFocus`, `MenuState.terminalInput`) only the commands with `inTerminal` fire; focus elsewhere in the terminal pane (a list row, the tab, Find, the rename field) is the terminal part for F6 and leaves every app key working (Quick Open, Show All Commands, Toggle Terminal, Toggle Sidebar, Settings, New Chat, F6 and Shift+F6, Split Terminal, Resize Pane on Linux and macOS, and Focus Previous and Next Pane only while the active group has more than one pane, as VS Code's `splitTerminalActive` gates them, so Alt+Left and Alt+Right reach the shell otherwise; `passKeys` follows the same `when` clauses through `Command.keyWhen`); every other key reaches the pty, as VS Code's `commandsToSkipShell` does; its menu item stays enabled, and `keyFires` refuses only the key. Links, OSC 8 hyperlinks included (xterm's `linkHandler`, never its `window.confirm` default), open on Ctrl+click (Cmd+click) through `ShellService.openExternal`, http and https only. The link parser finds the path before a suffix by one scan of the line, never by a regex anchored only at its end. A Linux middle click pastes the selection only while the program tracks no mouse (`modes.mouseTrackingMode === 'none'`) or Shift is held, as xterm decides. Clear marks a pending prompt again after `xterm.clear()` (VS Code's `clearBuffer`), and the command model disposes every marker it replaces or drops. `TerminalState.screenReader` (`app.isAccessibilitySupportEnabled()`, republished on `accessibility-support-changed`) sets xterm's `screenReaderMode`, and `TerminalState.windowsBuild` (`os.release()`) is `windowsPty.buildNumber`. A host exit is logged with its exit code, the reason Electron's `child-process-gone` gives for an abnormal exit (waited for at most 500 ms), and the tail of the host's stderr, which main pipes for this, or V8's fatal-error location.
- **A terminal tab is a group of side-by-side panes, and main owns the groups** (`terminal/terminal-service.ts`, VS Code's `terminalGroup.ts`). Every terminal is in exactly one group; `TerminalState.groups` lists them in order, each with its panes, active pane and sizes (fractions of at least `MIN_TERMINAL_PANE_FRACTION` summing to 1, at most `MAX_TERMINAL_GROUP_PANES` panes). Split Terminal starts a pane right of its source with the source's profile, looked up again among the listed profiles, in the folder the source's shell last reported while that is still a folder (`cwdOf` plus an async stat), else the project folder, and the new pane takes focus because the user asked. A split, a kill and an Unsplit leave equal sizes (VS Code's Sizing.Distribute); Unsplit moves a pane to a new group at the end of the list; a kill removes a pane, and with the last pane its group, and the active pane passes to the pane at the same index, else the last. Focus Previous and Next Pane wrap within the active group, Focus Next and Previous Terminal Group walk the groups, and Resize Pane asks the shell, which knows the cell width, for VS Code's four-cell step. Main takes a `resizePanes` only for a known group id with one finite size per pane, each at least the minimum, summing to 1 within `TERMINAL_PANE_SIZES_EPSILON`. The window layout keeps each group's pane count, active pane and sizes; saved groups that do not fit the saved list give each terminal its own group, with a log line, and the restore takes no focus. In the renderer the panes sit in one keyed list (`TerminalPanes.vue`), so a split, unsplit or kill never remounts an xterm, and only the active group's panes count as shown.
- **Terminal profiles of your own come from the user settings file only, and main validates every one** (`terminal/user-profiles.ts`, `terminal/executable-path.ts`, VS Code's `terminalProfiles.ts`). `damocles.desktop.terminal.profiles` is an `application` key, so the three-file merge never reads it from a project or local file, trusted or not, and `prefs:set` refuses it. An entry is `{ path, args?, icon?, color? }` keyed by its name; `null` hides the detected profile of that name, and an entry with a detected profile's name replaces it in place and keeps its id. Before any file system call main refuses an unknown field (there is no `env`), a UNC, device, drive-relative, root-relative, `~` or relative-with-folder path, and a Windows device name. Detected and user profiles alike search only the absolute local PATH entries of the terminals' environment (`searchPathEntries`), never main's working folder, and a user path counts only as a regular file after `followsLocalLinksOnly`. Args are NUL-free strings within the pty host's bounds and reach node-pty as argv. A refused entry never launches and is listed in Settings › Terminal with a `TERMINAL_PROFILE_PROBLEMS` reason; paths, args, names and problem text reach a renderer only as cleaned display copies. Create, split and restart look the profile id up among the listed profiles, so a hidden, removed or invalid profile never starts again. Shell integration and the WSL path style follow the executable, and main validates the setting again on every change of the user file. `setDetected` and `refresh` resolve only once the latest validation has applied, so the launch restore never reads the empty list a validation that was overtaken left.
- **Desktop-only packages never enter the VSIX closure.** `DESKTOP_ONLY_PACKAGES` in `scripts/sync-vscodeignore.mjs` is the desktop externals the extension lacks (`electron`, `@parcel/watcher`, `electron-updater`, `node-pty`), the desktop fonts (`@fontsource-variable/geist`, `@fontsource-variable/geist-mono`, `@fontsource-variable/inter`, whose files the shell build copies into `dist/desktop-shell/fonts/`) and the desktop build and test tools (`monaco-editor`, `@playwright/test`, `playwright`, `playwright-core`, `electron-builder`, `app-builder-lib`, `app-builder-bin`, `dmg-builder`), plus every package under the `DESKTOP_ONLY_PACKAGE_PREFIXES` `@electron/`, `@parcel/watcher-` and `@xterm/`; the closure walk fails when one of them reaches it.
- **Desktop files never enter the VSIX.** `DESKTOP_EXCLUDE_RULES` in the same script lists the `.vscodeignore` lines that keep out the desktop build (`dist/desktop/**`, `dist/desktop-shell/**`), electron-builder output (`dist-desktop/**`), the Monaco assets, the end-to-end suite and its output (`e2e/**`, `dist/e2e*/**`), the desktop build resources (`build/**`: the Linux package files and the macOS Icon Composer icon), the Windows icon (`resources/icon.ico`), typecheck stubs (`types/**`), the desktop configs and every `**/*.log`. `--check` fails when a rule is missing or a negation could re-include one of them, and `--check-vsix-listing` checks the file list of a real built VSIX on every release leg.
- **The Electron binary is never downloaded by `npm ci`.** `electron` has no install script: its `index.js` downloads the binary the first time something requires it. So the VSIX jobs never fetch it, and anything that runs Electron downloads it first with the package's own idempotent `install-electron` bin: `npm run test:desktop` and `npm run dev:desktop` do, and the release workflow's `package-desktop` legs do right after `npm ci`, because the Linux legs make `node_modules/electron/dist/chrome-sandbox` setuid before any test runs. Without it, two Playwright workers race to download and extract into the same `dist/`.
- **The desktop Monaco editor never enters the VSIX or the VS Code webview's eager bundle.** Everything reachable only through the editor overlay's dynamic import (`src/webview/components/editor/EditorOverlayHost.vue`) is emitted as `dist/webview/assets/monaco-*`: JS chunks, the per-language tokenizer chunks, both workers, the extracted CSS and the codicon font; the settings schema is inlined into those chunks. `vite.config.ts` computes that set from the module graph, and its build fails when a chunk mixes Monaco-only and eager modules, when a non-prefixed chunk statically loads a `monaco-` file or bundles a `monaco-editor` module, or when a prefixed chunk loads an unprefixed CSS or asset file. `.vscodeignore` excludes `dist/webview/assets/monaco-*`, and `sync-vscodeignore.mjs` (both modes) fails when that rule is missing or a negation could re-include a Monaco file. Every URL Vite writes into JS is relative to the file that holds it (`experimental.renderBuiltUrl`), because the desktop app serves the bundle under `app://damocles/webview/` and a root-absolute `/assets/` URL (the default for base `/`) misses it; that is also why the preload helper takes `import.meta.url` at every lazy call site. The root `dompurify` range stays `~3.3.2` so `monaco-editor`'s exact `dompurify` pin nests under it instead of replacing the copy the VS Code webview bundles.
- **The settings schema is committed and checked, not generated per build only.** `npm run build:webview` regenerates it and `scripts/__tests__/generate-settings-schema.test.ts` fails when the committed file is stale, so `tsc` resolves the import on a fresh clone. The generator imports `parseContributedConfiguration` from `src/core/config/contributed-configuration.ts` through Node's type stripping, so that file must stay erasable TypeScript with `node:` imports only.
- **Attribution ships inside the VSIX and the desktop app.** `.vscodeignore` excludes `*.md` and re-includes `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` outside the generated block; `electron-builder.yml` packs `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` into `app.asar` and copies Monaco's `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` into `resources/`, and `scripts/verify-desktop-package.mjs` fails a package without them. A library that esbuild or Vite bundles ships no `node_modules` copy of its license, so adding one means adding its entry to `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`. The bundled word lists' credits (CC BY and MIT) are `/*!` legal comments emitted by `scripts/generate-reference-words.mjs`, which esbuild keeps in the minified bundle. Setting esbuild's `legalComments` to `none` or turning that header back into `//` comments strips that credit from the bundle.
- **The closure walk follows Node resolution, so it must climb the whole ancestor `node_modules` chain.** Checking the immediate parent and then jumping to the project root reports the hoisted top-level copy for any dependency npm nested to satisfy a version conflict, and that copy can be a devDependency the extension never loads. That is how a 10 MB Windows compiler binary reached the VSIX: `@earendil-works/chord` requires `esbuild@0.28.2`, npm nests it under `@earendil-works/chord`, and a walk that skips that level reports our build-time `esbuild@0.24.2` instead. `assertNoDevOnlyPackages` fails the run when a devDependencies-only package reaches the closure, and names the package that asked for it.
